WEEK 8: QUADRATIC EQUATIONS II
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
Form quadratic equations from given conditions
Find relationships between roots and coefficients
Verify given roots of quadratic equations
Solve word problems involving quadratic equations
Apply quadratic equations to real-life situations
Analyze the nature of roots using the discriminant
Entry Behaviour:
Students can solve quadratic equations by various methods and understand the relationship between roots and coefficients.
Instructional Materials:
Whiteboard and markers
Calculator
Real-life scenario cards
Graph paper
Formula charts
Content:
Forming Equations from Given Conditions
Diagram 93: Types of Conditions
CONDITION TYPE 1: Given specific roots

Example: Roots are 3 and -4 Solution: (x - 3)(x + 4) = 0
x² + x - 12 = 0
────────────────────────────────── CONDITION TYPE 2: Given sum and product Example: Sum = 7, Product = 10
Formula: x² - (sum)x + (product) = 0 Solution: x² - 7x + 10 = 0

──────────────────────────────────
CONDITION TYPE 3: Roots related to each other Example: One root is twice the other
Let roots be α and 2α

Given additional info to find α Then form equation
────────────────────────────────── CONDITION TYPE 4: Roots differ by a constant Example: Roots differ by 3
If roots are α and β:
β - α = 3 or α - β = 3

Use with sum/product to find values

────────────────────────────────── DECISION TREE:
Given Condition
│
┌────┴────┐
│	│
Specific	Relationship Roots		Between Roots
│	│
Use	Use algebra Factors	to find roots
│	│
└────┬────┘
│
Form Equation
Diagram 94: Complex Root Relationships
CASE 1: One root is k times the other Let roots be α and kα
Sum: α + kα = α(1 + k) Product: α × kα = kα²

Example: One root is 3 times the other Sum = 12

Let roots be α and 3α α + 3α = 12
4α = 12
α = 3

Roots: 3 and 9
Equation: (x - 3)(x - 9) = 0
x² - 12x + 27 = 0

VISUAL:
α	3α
●	●●●
└──┬──┘
Sum = 4α = 12
────────────────────────────────── CASE 2: Roots differ by a constant Let roots be α and α + d
(where d is the difference)

Sum: α + (α + d) = 2α + d Product: α(α + d) = α² + αd

Example: Roots differ by 4 Sum = 10
Let roots be α and α + 4 α + (α + 4) = 10
2α + 4 = 10
2α = 6
α = 3

Roots: 3 and 7
Equation: (x - 3)(x - 7) = 0
x² - 10x + 21 = 0

NUMBER LINE:
3	7
●───────●
└───4───┘ (difference)

────────────────────────────────── CASE 3: Roots are reciprocals
If roots are α and 1/α Product: α × 1/α = 1
Example: Roots are reciprocals
Sum = 5/2

Let roots be α and 1/α α + 1/α = 5/2

Multiply by α:
α² + 1 = 5α/2 2α² + 2 = 5α
2α² - 5α + 2 = 0

Solve: (2α - 1)(α - 2) = 0 α = 1/2 or α = 2

Roots: 1/2 and 2 (reciprocals ✓)
────────────────────────────────── CASE 4: Roots are opposites
If roots are α and -α Sum: α + (-α) = 0
Example: Roots are opposites
Product = -16

Let roots be α and -α α × (-α) = -16
-α² = -16
α² = 16
α = ±4

Roots: 4 and -4
Equation: x² - 16 = 0

VISUAL:
-α	α
●────┼────●
0
↑	↑
Opposite on number line

────────────────────────────────── CASE 5: Sum of squares given
If α² + β² is known:

Use identity:
(α + β)² = α² + 2αβ + β² α² + β² = (α + β)² - 2αβ

Example: α² + β² = 13
α + β = 5
13 = 5² - 2αβ
13 = 25 - 2αβ
2αβ = 12
αβ = 6

Sum = 5, Product = 6 Equation: x² - 5x + 6 = 0

FORMULA BOX:
┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ α² + β² = (α+β)² - 2αβ	│
│ α³ + β³ = (α+β)³ - 3αβ(α+β) │
│ (α-β)² = (α+β)² - 4αβ	│
└─────────────────────────────┘

Verification of Roots
Diagram 95: Root Verification Methods
METHOD 1: Direct Substitution

Given: x² - 5x + 6 = 0
Verify: x = 2 and x = 3 are roots

For x = 2:
2² - 5(2) + 6
= 4 - 10 + 6
= 0 ✓

For x = 3:
3² - 5(3) + 6
= 9 - 15 + 6
= 0 ✓

VERIFICATION CHECKLIST:
Substitute value
Follow order of operations
Should equal zero
Check both roots

──────────────────────────────────

METHOD 2: Using Sum and Product

Given: 2x² + 5x - 3 = 0
Verify: x = 1/2 and x = -3 are roots

From equation:
a = 2, b = 5, c = -3

Sum should be: -b/a = -5/2
Check: 1/2 + (-3) = 1/2 - 6/2 = -5/2 ✓

Product should be: c/a = -3/2 Check: (1/2)×(-3) = -3/2 ✓

FORMULA TABLE:
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ Given Equation │ Check	│
├─────────────────┼────────────┤
│ ax²+bx+c = 0	│ α+β = -b/a │
│	│ αβ = c/a	│
└──────────────────────────────┘
────────────────────────────────── METHOD 3: Factorization Check Given: x² - 7x + 12 = 0
Verify: Roots are 3 and 4

Factor: (x - 3)(x - 4) Expand: x² - 4x - 3x + 12
= x² - 7x + 12 ✓
Matches original equation VISUAL:
Equation → Factor → Roots
x²-7x+12 (x-3)(x-4) 3,4
↓	↓	↓
Expand ← Check → Verify
────────────────────────────────── METHOD 4: Graphical Verification Plot y = ax² + bx + c
Roots are where graph crosses x-axis Example: y = x² - 5x + 6
y
│	╱\
│	╱ \
6 │──╱	\
│ ╱	\
│╱	\		x 0 2		3

Crosses at x = 2 and x = 3 ✓

VERIFICATION ERRORS TO AVOID:
✗ Arithmetic mistakes
✗ Sign errors
✗ Forgetting negative roots
✗ Not checking both roots
✗ Calculation without showing work

Nature of Roots (Discriminant) Diagram 96: Discriminant Analysis THE DISCRIMINANT (Δ)
For ax² + bx + c = 0:

┌─────────────────────────┐
│ Δ = b² - 4ac	│
└─────────────────────────┘ DETERMINES:
Number of roots
Type of roots
Nature of roots
────────────────────────────────── CASE 1: Δ > 0
Two distinct real roots

Example: x² - 5x + 6 = 0 a = 1, b = -5, c = 6
Δ = (-5)² - 4(1)(6) Δ = 25 - 24 = 1 > 0

Graph:
╱\
╱ \
_╱	\
↑	↑
Two crossings
────────────────────────────────── CASE 2: Δ = 0
Two equal roots (one repeated root)

Example: x² - 4x + 4 = 0 a = 1, b = -4, c = 4
Δ = (-4)² - 4(1)(4) Δ = 16 - 16 = 0

Graph:
╱\
╱ \
_╱	\
↑
One touching point
────────────────────────────────── CASE 3: Δ < 0
No real roots (complex roots)

Example: x² + 2x + 5 = 0 a = 1, b = 2, c = 5
Δ = 2² - 4(1)(5)
Δ = 4 - 20 = -16 < 0

Graph:
╱\
╱ \
╱	\
╱	\
────────── No crossings
────────────────────────────────── SPECIAL CASE: Perfect Square
When Δ = k² (perfect square)
Roots are rational

Example: 2x² + 7x + 3 = 0 Δ = 7² - 4(2)(3)
Δ = 49 - 24 = 25 = 5²

Roots will be rational: x = (-7 ± 5)/4
x = -1/2 or x = -3

DISCRIMINANT TABLE:
┌──────────┬─────────────────┐
│	Δ	│	Nature	│
├──────────┼─────────────────┤

│> 0│ 2 distinct real ││= 0│ 2 equal roots	││< 0│No real roots││=k²│Rational roots││≠k²│Irrational roots│└──────────┴─────────────────┘

DECISION TREE:
Calculate Δ
│
┌─────┼─────┐

│││>0=0<0│││2 real 1 real No real distinct equal roots roots root

VISUAL SUMMARY:
Δ > 0: ╱\	/\
Crosses twice

Δ = 0: ╱\	•
Touches once

Δ < 0: ╱\
Never crosses

Word Problems with Quadratic Equations Diagram 97: Problem-Solving Framework STEPS FOR WORD PROBLEMS:
READ carefully
IDENTIFY what to find
DEFINE variables
SET UP equation
SOLVE equation
CHECK answer
STATE conclusion PROBLEM TYPES:

┌────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. NUMBER PROBLEMS	│
│ 2. AGE PROBLEMS	│
│ 3. AREA/PERIMETER	│
│ 4. MOTION/DISTANCE	│
│ 5. WORK/TIME	│
│ 6. BUSINESS/PROFIT	│
└────────────────────────────┘

TRANSLATION GUIDE:
"sum of" → + "product of" → × "difference of" → - "quotient of" → ÷ "is/equals" → = "more than" → + "less than" → - "times" → × "squared" → ²
Diagram 98: Number Problems
PROBLEM TYPE 1: Number and Square

Problem: The square of a number exceeds
the number by 12. Find the number.

STEP 1: Define variable
Let the number be x

STEP 2: Set up equation
"square exceeds number by 12" x² = x + 12

STEP 3: Rearrange to standard form x² - x - 12 = 0

STEP 4: Solve by factorization
Need factors of -12 that add to -1 Factors: -4 and 3

(x - 4)(x + 3) = 0
x = 4 or x = -3

STEP 5: Check both solutions For x = 4:
4² = 16, 4 + 12 = 16 ✓

For x = -3:
(-3)² = 9, -3 + 12 = 9 ✓

STEP 6: State answer
The number is 4 or -3

VISUAL:
Number: x Square: x²
Exceeds by 12: x² = x + 12

x	x+12
●─────●───────● x²
────────────────────────────────── PROBLEM TYPE 2: Sum and Product Problem: Two numbers differ by 3 and
their product is 70. Find them.

STEP 1: Define variables
Let smaller number be x Larger number is x + 3

STEP 2: Set up equation
"product is 70"
x(x + 3) = 70

STEP 3: Expand and rearrange x² + 3x = 70
x² + 3x - 70 = 0

STEP 4: Solve
(x + 10)(x - 7) = 0
x = -10 or x = 7

STEP 5: Find both numbers If x = 7:
Numbers are 7 and 10
Check: 10 - 7 = 3 ✓
7 × 10 = 70 ✓

If x = -10:
Numbers are -10 and -7
Check: -7 - (-10) = 3 ✓
-10 × -7 = 70 ✓

ANSWER: The numbers are 7 and 10, or -10 and -7

NUMBER LINE:
-10	-7	0	7	10
●────────●	│	●──────●
└───3────┘	└──3──┘ Diagram 99: Area Problems PROBLEM: Rectangle Area
Problem: A rectangle has length 3 cm more than its width. Its area is 40 cm². Find the dimensions.

STEP 1: Define variable
Let width = w cm Length = (w + 3) cm

VISUAL:
┌──────────────┐
│	│ w
│	│
└──────────────┘ w + 3

STEP 2: Set up equation
Area = length × width 40 = (w + 3) × w
40 = w² + 3w

STEP 3: Standard form
w² + 3w - 40 = 0

STEP 4: Factorize
(w + 8)(w - 5) = 0
w = -8 or w = 5

STEP 5: Choose valid solution Width cannot be negative w = 5 cm
Length = 5 + 3 = 8 cm

STEP 6: Verify
Area = 8 × 5 = 40 cm² ✓
ANSWER: Width = 5 cm, Length = 8 cm AREA MODEL:
┌────────┐
5 │////////│ 8
│////////│
└────────┘ Area = 40 cm²

────────────────────────────────── PROBLEM: Triangle Area
Problem: The base of a triangle is 4 cm longer than its height. If the
area is 30 cm², find base and height.
STEP 1: Define variables
Let height = h cm Base = (h + 4) cm

VISUAL:
/\
/ \ h
/	\
/	\ h+4

STEP 2: Use area formula
Area = ½ × base × height 30 = ½ × (h + 4) × h
60 = h(h + 4)
60 = h² + 4h

STEP 3: Standard form
h² + 4h - 60 = 0

STEP 4: Factorize
(h + 10)(h - 6) = 0
h = -10 or h = 6

STEP 5: Valid solution
Height cannot be negative h = 6 cm
Base = 6 + 4 = 10 cm

STEP 6: Verify
Area = ½ × 10 × 6 = 30 cm² ✓

ANSWER: Height = 6 cm, Base = 10 cm

Diagram 100: Motion Problems
PROBLEM: Speed and Distance

Problem: A car travels 180 km. If the speed was 15 km/h faster, the journey would take 1 hour less.
Find the original speed.

STEP 1: Define variable
Let original speed = v km/h New speed = (v + 15) km/h

STEP 2: Use Time = Distance/Speed Original time = 180/v hours New time = 180/(v+15) hours

STEP 3: Set up equation
"1 hour less"
180/v - 180/(v+15) = 1

STEP 4: Solve
Multiply by v(v+15): 180(v+15) - 180v = v(v+15)
180v + 2700 - 180v = v² + 15v
2700 = v² + 15v
v² + 15v - 2700 = 0

STEP 5: Use quadratic formula
v = (-15 ± √(225 + 10800))/2 v = (-15 ± √11025)/2
v = (-15 ± 105)/2
v = 45 or v = -60

STEP 6: Valid solution
Speed cannot be negative v = 45 km/h

VERIFICATION:
Original time: 180/45 = 4 hours New time: 180/60 = 3 hours Difference: 4 - 3 = 1 hour ✓
ANSWER: Original speed = 45 km/h TIME-SPEED TABLE:
┌─────────────┬──────┬──────┐
│	│Time │Speed │
├─────────────┼──────┼──────┤
│ Original	│180/v │ v	│
│ New	│180/ │ v+15 │
│	│(v+15)│	│
│ Difference │ 1	│ 15 │
└─────────────┴──────┴──────┘

Worked Examples with Complete Solutions:
Example 1: One root of 2x² + kx - 6 = 0 is 2. Find k and the other root.
Diagram 101: Finding Unknown Coefficient
Given: 2x² + kx - 6 = 0
One root is x = 2 METHOD 1: Direct Substitution
STEP 1: Substitute x = 2
2(2)² + k(2) - 6 = 0
2(4) + 2k - 6 = 0
8 + 2k - 6 = 0
2k + 2 = 0
2k = -2
k = -1

STEP 2: Find other root
Equation becomes: 2x² - x - 6 = 0

Using sum of roots:
α + β = -b/a = -(-1)/2 = 1/2

Since α = 2:
2 + β = 1/2
β = 1/2 - 2 = -3/2

────────────────────────────────── METHOD 2: Using Product of Roots
STEP 1: Use product formula
α × β = c/a = -6/2 = -3

Since α = 2:
2 × β = -3
β = -3/2

STEP 2: Use sum to find k
α + β = -k/2
2 + (-3/2) = -k/2
1/2 = -k/2 k = -1

VERIFICATION:
Equation: 2x² - x - 6 = 0

For x = 2:
2(2)² - 2 - 6 = 8 - 2 - 6 = 0 ✓

For x = -3/2:
2(-3/2)² - (-3/2) - 6
= 2(9/4) + 3/2 - 6
= 9/2 + 3/2 - 12/2
= 0 ✓

VISUAL SUMMARY:
2x² + kx - 6 = 0
↓ (x=2)
8 + 2k - 6 = 0
↓
k = -1
↓
2x² - x - 6 = 0
↓
Roots: 2 and -3/2
ANSWER: k = -1, other root = -3/2 NUMBER LINE:
────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼────→
-2	-3/202↑↑Root 2Root 1
Example 2: The roots of x² - px + 12 = 0 are in the ratio 3:4. Find p and the roots.
Diagram 102: Ratio Root Problem
Given: x² - px + 12 = 0
Roots in ratio 3:4

STEP 1: Express roots
Let roots be 3k and 4k (where k is a constant)

RATIO VISUAL:
3k	4k
●●●	●●●●
└──┬──────┘ Ratio 3:4

STEP 2: Use product of roots Product = c/a = 12/1 = 12

3k × 4k = 12
12k² = 12
k² = 1
k = ±1

STEP 3: Find actual roots If k = 1:
Roots are 3 and 4

If k = -1:
Roots are -3 and -4

STEP 4: Find p using sum
Case 1: Roots are 3 and 4 Sum = -(-p)/1 = p
3 + 4 = p
p = 7

Case 2: Roots are -3 and -4 Sum = p
-3 + (-4) = p
p = -7

VERIFICATION:
For p = 7: x² - 7x + 12 = 0 Factor: (x - 3)(x - 4) = 0 Roots: 3 and 4
Ratio: 3:4 ✓
Product: 3×4 = 12 ✓

For p = -7: x² + 7x + 12 = 0 Factor: (x + 3)(x + 4) = 0 Roots: -3 and -4
Ratio: -3:-4 = 3:4 ✓
Product: (-3)×(-4) = 12 ✓

COMPLETE SOLUTION TABLE:
┌─────┬────────┬──────────┐
│ p │ Roots │	Check │
├─────┼────────┼──────────┤

└─────┴────────┴──────────┘ ANSWER:
p = 7 with roots 3 and 4, OR
p = -7 with roots -3 and -4

VISUAL RATIO:
Positive case:
├───┼────┤
3	4
parts : 4 parts

Negative case:
├────┼───┤
-4 -3
parts : 3 parts (same ratio)

Example 3: The perimeter of a rectangle is 26 cm and its area is 40 cm². Find its dimensions.
Diagram 103: Rectangle Problem
Given:
Perimeter = 26 cm Area = 40 cm²

STEP 1: Define variables
Let length = l cm Let width = w cm

RECTANGLE VISUAL:
┌──────────────┐
│	│ w
│	│
└──────────────┘
l

STEP 2: Set up equations
Perimeter: 2(l + w) = 26
l + w = 13 ... (1)

Area: l × w = 40 ... (2)

STEP 3: Express l in terms of w From (1): l = 13 - w

STEP 4: Substitute into (2) (13 - w) × w = 40
13w - w² = 40
-w² + 13w - 40 = 0 w² - 13w + 40 = 0

STEP 5: Factorize
Need factors of 40 that add to -13

Factors: -8 and -5 (w - 8)(w - 5) = 0
w = 8 or w = 5

STEP 6: Find corresponding lengths If w = 8: l = 13 - 8 = 5
If w = 5: l = 13 - 5 = 8

STEP 7: Interpret result
Dimensions are 8 cm × 5 cm (Same rectangle, just swapping length and width)

VERIFICATION:
Perimeter: 2(8 + 5) = 26 ✓
Area: 8 × 5 = 40 ✓

SOLUTION DIAGRAM:
┌────────────────┐
5 │////////////////│ 8
│////////////////│
└────────────────┘ 8

Perimeter = 2(8+5) = 26 cm
Area = 8 × 5 = 40 cm²

DIMENSIONS TABLE:
┌────────┬────────┬──────────┬──────┐
│ Length │ Width │Perimeter │ Area │
├────────┼────────┼──────────┼──────┤

└────────┴────────┴──────────┴──────┘ ANSWER: Length = 8 cm, Width = 5 cm
(or vice versa)

ALGEBRAIC SOLUTION PATH:
l + w = 13 l × w = 40
↓
l = 13 - w
↓
(13-w)×w = 40
↓
w² - 13w + 40 = 0
↓
(w-8)(w-5) = 0
↓
w = 8 or 5
↓ Dimensions: 8×5 cm
Example 4: A man is 24 years older than his son. In 4 years, the product of their ages will be
360. Find their present ages.
Diagram 104: Age Problem
Given:
Man is 24 years older than son
In 4 years: product of ages = 360

STEP 1: Define variables
Let son's present age = x years Man's present age = (x + 24) years

AGE TABLE NOW:
┌──────────┬─────────────┐
│	Son	│	Man	│
├──────────┼─────────────┤
│	x	│	x + 24	│
└──────────┴─────────────┘ AGE TABLE IN 4 YEARS:
┌──────────┬─────────────┐
│	Son	│	Man	│
├──────────┼─────────────┤
│ x + 4	│ x + 28	│
└──────────┴─────────────┘

STEP 2: Set up equation
"In 4 years, product = 360" (x + 4)(x + 28) = 360

STEP 3: Expand
x² + 28x + 4x + 112 = 360 x² + 32x + 112 = 360

STEP 4: Standard form
x² + 32x + 112 - 360 = 0 x² + 32x - 248 = 0

STEP 5: Use quadratic formula
a = 1, b = 32, c = -248

x = (-32 ± √(32² - 4(1)(-248)))/2 x = (-32 ± √(1024 + 992))/2
x = (-32 ± √2016)/2 x = (-32 ± 44.9)/2

x = 12.9/2 or -76.9/2
x ≈ 6.45 or x ≈ -38.45

STEP 6: Choose valid solution Age cannot be negative
x ≈ 6 years (rounding to integer)

Actually, let's solve more carefully: x² + 32x - 248 = 0

Try factoring:
(x + 38)(x - 6) = 0? Check: 38 × -6 = -228 ✗
Let's try: (x - 6)(x + ?) Need: -6 × ? = -248
? = 248/6 ≈ 41.33

Not exact factors, use formula: x = (-32 ± √2016)/2

But √2016 = √(16×126) = 4√126

Actually, checking if 248 factors: 248 = 8 × 31

Trying (x - 6)(x + 38): x² + 38x - 6x - 228
= x² + 32x - 228 ✗

Let me recalculate discriminant: Δ = 1024 + 992 = 2016

Since not perfect square, use approximation:
x ≈ 6 years

STEP 7: Find man's age
Man's age = 6 + 24 = 30 years

VERIFICATION (approximate): In 4 years:
Son: 6 + 4 = 10 years
Man: 30 + 4 = 34 years
Product: 10 × 34 = 340 ≈ 360 (Close enough with rounding)

For exact solution: If (x+4)(x+28) = 360 x² + 32x + 112 = 360 x² + 32x - 248 = 0

Using completing square: x² + 32x = 248
x² + 32x + 256 = 504 (x + 16)² = 504
x + 16 = ±√504 x = -16 ± √504
x = -16 + 22.45 = 6.45
x ≈ 6 years

TIMELINE VISUAL:
Now	In 4 years Son: 6 ────────→ 10
↕24	↕24
Man: 30 ────────→ 34

Product in 4 years: 10×34 = 340

(Note: Slight discrepancy due to rounding. Exact answer requires keeping √504 form)

ANSWER:
Son's present age ≈ 6 years Man's present age ≈ 30 years

Or exactly:
Son: -16 + √504 ≈ 6.45 years Man: 8 + √504 ≈ 30.45 years
Example 5: Determine the nature of roots of 3x² - 5x + 4 = 0 without solving.
Diagram 105: Discriminant Analysis
Given: 3x² - 5x + 4 = 0

STEP 1: Identify coefficients a = 3
b = -5
c = 4

COEFFICIENT BOX:
┌─────────────────┐
│ a = 3 (x² term) │
│ b = -5 (x term) │
│ c = 4 (const)	│
└─────────────────┘

STEP 2: Calculate discriminant Δ = b² - 4ac
Δ = (-5)² - 4(3)(4) Δ = 25 - 48
Δ = -23

CALCULATION BREAKDOWN: b² = (-5)² = 25
4ac= 4 × 3× 4 = 48Δ =25 - 48= -23STEP 3:Analyze Δ = -23result
< 0
DISCRIMINANT DECISION: Δ < 0
↓
NO REAL ROOTS
↓ Complex/Imaginary
roots

STEP 4: Graphical interpretation Since Δ < 0, the parabola does not cross the x-axis

GRAPH:
y
│	╱\
│	╱ \
│	╱	\
│ ╱	\
│ ╱	\
│╱	\	x
│
Never crosses x-axis

NATURE OF ROOTS:
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ • Two complex conjugate roots│
│ • Form: p ± qi	│
│ • No real solutions	│
│ • Parabola entirely above	│
│	or below x-axis	│
└──────────────────────────────┘

COMPARISON TABLE:
┌────────┬──────────┬───────────┐
│	Δ	│ Value	│ Nature	│
├────────┼──────────┼───────────┤
│ > 0	│ Positive │ 2 real	│
│ = 0	│	Zero	│ 1 real	│
│ < 0	│ Negative │ No real ✓ │
└────────┴──────────┴───────────┘

ANSWER:
The equation has NO REAL ROOTS (two complex conjugate roots)
Because: Δ = -23 < 0 VISUAL SUMMARY:
3x² - 5x + 4 = 0
↓
Δ = -23
↓
Δ < 0
↓
No real roots
↓
╱\
╱ \ (graph above x-axis)
╱	\
Class Activity:
Activity 1: Root Finding Challenge
Diagram 106: Practice Problems
Find the value of k in each case:

x² + kx + 9 = 0 has equal roots Hint: Δ = 0

Solution:
Δ = k² - 4(1)(9) = 0 k² - 36 = 0
k = ±6
kx² - 6x + 2 = 0 has one root as 2 Solution:
k(2)² - 6(2) + 2 = 0
4k - 12 + 2 = 0
4k = 10
k = 5/2

2x² + 5x + k = 0 has product of roots as 3

Solution:
Product = c/a = k/2 k/2 = 3
k = 6

Age relationships

Activity 3: Discriminant Detective
Without solving, determine the nature of roots:
1. x² - 4x + 4 = 0 (Δ = 0, equal roots)
2.  2x² + 3x - 5 = 0 (Δ = 49 > 0, 2 real)
3. x² + x + 1 = 0 (Δ = -3 < 0, no real)
Evaluation Questions:
One root of x² + px + 8 = 0 is 4. Find p and the other root.

Form an equation whose roots are 5 and -3.

The sum of two numbers is 12 and their product is 35. Find the numbers.

WEEK 9: SIMULTANEOUS EQUATIONS
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
Define simultaneous equations
Solve simultaneous linear equations by elimination method
Solve simultaneous equations by substitution method
Solve one linear and one quadratic equation simultaneously
Apply simultaneous equations to real-life problems
Choose the most appropriate method for different types
Entry Behaviour:
Students can solve linear equations, expand brackets, and perform basic algebraic manipulations.
Instructional Materials:
Whiteboard and markers
Algebra tiles or manipulatives
Graph paper
Calculator
Worked examples charts
Practice worksheets
Content:
Introduction to Simultaneous Equations
Definition: Simultaneous equations are two or more equations that share common variables and must be solved together to find values that satisfy all equations at once.
Diagram 107: Understanding Simultaneous Equations
WHAT ARE SIMULTANEOUS EQUATIONS?

Two equations with two unknowns (x and y):
┌────────────────────────┐
│ Equation 1: ax + by = c│
│ Equation 2: dx + ey = f│
└────────────────────────┘
Solution: Values of x and y that make BOTH equations true

VISUAL CONCEPT:

Equation 1: 2x + y = 7
Points on line: (0,7), (1,5), (2,3), (3,1)

Equation 2: x - y = 2
Points on line: (0,-2), (1,-1), (2,0), (3,1)

y
│	Line 1
7 ●
│ \
5 │	●
│	\
3 │	●
│	\ Line 2
1 │	●───────
│	/ \
-1 │	●	\
│	/	\
-2 │	●	\
└────────────────── x 0	1	2	3

Intersection point (3, 1) is the solution! x = 3, y = 1 satisfies BOTH equations

VERIFICATION:
Equation 1: 2(3) + 1 = 6 + 1 = 7 ✓
Equation 2: 3 - 1 = 2 ✓

────────────────────────────────── TYPES OF SOLUTIONS:
ONE UNIQUE SOLUTION
Lines intersect at one point

/\
/ \
/	\
─────── (intersection)

NO SOLUTION
Parallel lines (never meet)

═══════

═══════

INFINITE SOLUTIONS Same line (coincident)

═══════ (both equations
represent same line) STANDARD FORMS:
Linear-Linear:
┌────────────────────┐
│ ax + by = c	│
│ dx + ey = f	│
└────────────────────┘

Linear-Quadratic:
┌────────────────────┐
│ y = mx + c	│
│ y = ax² + bx + c	│
└────────────────────┘

Elimination Method
Diagram 108: Elimination Method Steps
PRINCIPLE: Eliminate one variable by making coefficients equal

GENERAL STEPS:
┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. Write equations clearly	│
│ 2. Make coefficients equal	│
│ 3. Add or subtract equations	│
│ 4. Solve for one variable	│
│ 5. Substitute back	│
│ 6. Find other variable	│
│ 7. Verify solution	│
└────────────────────────────────┘ STRATEGY GUIDE:
Look at coefficients:
┌──────────────────────────────┐
│ Same coefficient already?	│
│ → Directly add/subtract	│
│	│
│ Different coefficients?	│
│ → Multiply to make equal	│
│	│
│ One coefficient is 1?	│
│ → Good candidate for subst. │
└──────────────────────────────┘

SIGN RULES:
Same signs → Subtract equations
Different signs → Add equations

To eliminate:
+ with - → ADD
+ with + → SUBTRACT
with - → SUBTRACT
with + → ADD

Diagram 109: Elimination - Simple Case
EXAMPLE: Coefficients already equal

Solve: 2x + 3y = 13 ... (1) 2x - y = 5	... (2)
STEP 1: Identify equal coefficients Both have 2x

VISUAL:
Eq 1: 2x + 3y = 13 Eq 2: 2x - y = 5
↑↑
Same coefficient!

STEP 2: Subtract to eliminate x (1) - (2):

2x + 3y = 13
-(2x - y = 5)
───────────── 0 + 4y = 8

SUBTRACTION BREAKDOWN:
┌──────────────────────────┐
│ 2x - 2x = 0	│
│ 3y - (-y) = 3y + y = 4y │
│ 13 - 5 = 8	│
└──────────────────────────┘

STEP 3: Solve for y
4y = 8
y = 2

STEP 4: Substitute y = 2 into (1)
2x + 3(2) = 13
2x + 6 = 13
2x = 7
x = 7/2 = 3.5

SOLUTION BOX:
┌──────────────┐
│ x = 3.5	│
│ y = 2	│
└──────────────┘
VERIFICATION:
Equation 1: 2(3.5) + 3(2) = 7 + 6 = 13 ✓
Equation 2: 2(3.5) - 2 = 7 - 2 = 5 ✓

VISUAL SOLUTION PROCESS: 2x + 3y = 13
2x - y = 5
↓ (subtract) 4y = 8
↓ (÷4)
y = 2
↓ (substitute) 2x + 6 = 13
↓
x = 3.5

Diagram 110: Elimination - Multiplying First
EXAMPLE: Making coefficients equal

Solve: 3x + 2y = 16 ... (1) 2x + 5y = 21 ... (2)

STEP 1: Choose variable to eliminate Let's eliminate x

STEP 2: Find LCM of x-coefficients Coefficients: 3 and 2
LCM = 6

MULTIPLICATION PLAN:
┌─────────────────────────┐
│ Eq 1 × 2 → 6x + 4y = 32 │
│ Eq 2 × 3 → 6x + 15y = 63│
└─────────────────────────┘

STEP 3: Multiply equations
(1) × 2: 6x + 4y = 32 ... (3)
(2) × 3: 6x + 15y = 63 ... (4)

STEP 4: Subtract to eliminate x
(4) - (3):

6x + 15y = 63
-(6x + 4y = 32)
────────────── 0 + 11y = 31

SUBTRACTION VISUAL: 6x + 15y = 63
-(6x + 4y = 32)
─────────────── 0 + 11y = 31

STEP 5: Solve for y
11y = 31 y = 31/11

STEP 6: Substitute into original (1) 3x + 2(31/11) = 16
3x + 62/11 = 16
3x = 16 - 62/11
3x = (176 - 62)/11
3x = 114/11
x = 114/33 = 38/11

EXACT ANSWER:
┌──────────────────┐
│ x = 38/11	│
│ y = 31/11	│
└──────────────────┘

DECIMAL APPROXIMATION:
┌──────────────────┐
│ x ≈ 3.45	│
│ y ≈ 2.82	│
└──────────────────┘

VERIFICATION (using decimals):
Eq 1: 3(3.45) + 2(2.82) ≈ 10.35 + 5.64 = 15.99 ≈ 16 ✓
Eq 2: 2(3.45) + 5(2.82) ≈ 6.90 + 14.10 = 21.00 ✓

SOLUTION PATH: 3x + 2y = 16
2x + 5y = 21
↓ (×2, ×3) 6x + 4y = 32
6x + 15y = 63
↓ (subtract) 11y = 31
↓ (÷11) y = 31/11
↓ (substitute) x = 38/11
Substitution Method
Diagram 111: Substitution Method Steps
PRINCIPLE: Express one variable in terms of the other, then substitute

GENERAL STEPS:
┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. Choose simpler equation	│
│ 2. Express one variable	│
│ 3. Substitute into other eq.	│
│ 4. Solve resulting equation	│
│ 5. Find other variable	│
│ 6. Verify solution	│
└────────────────────────────────┘

WHEN TO USE SUBSTITUTION:
One equation already solved for a variable
Coefficient of 1 present
One equation easily rearranged
Non-linear equations (one quadratic)
DECISION GUIDE:
Look at equations
│
┌──────┴──────┐
│	│
Coefficient	All different of 1?	coefficients?
│	│
↓	↓
Substitution Elimination better	better
Diagram 112: Substitution - Basic Example
EXAMPLE: Simple substitution

Solve: y = 2x - 1	... (1)
3x + 2y = 12 ... (2)

STEP 1: Identify substitution
Equation (1) already has y isolated! y = 2x - 1

STEP 2: Substitute into equation (2) 3x + 2(2x - 1) = 12

SUBSTITUTION VISUAL: 3x + 2y = 12
↓
3x + 2(2x-1) = 12
└─────┘ Replace y

STEP 3: Expand and simplify 3x + 4x - 2 = 12
7x - 2 = 12
7x = 14
x = 2

STEP 4: Find y using equation (1) y = 2(2) - 1
y = 4 - 1
y = 3

SOLUTION:
┌──────────────┐
│ x = 2	│
│ y = 3	│
└──────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Eq 1: y = 2(2) - 1 = 3 ✓
Eq 2: 3(2) + 2(3) = 6 + 6 = 12 ✓

SOLUTION FLOW:
y = 2x - 1 3x + 2y = 12
↓
3x + 2(2x-1) = 12
↓
7x = 14
↓
x = 2
↓
y = 2(2) - 1
↓
y = 3

NUMBER LINE CHECK:
For x = 2, y = 3:
x: ───┼─────┼────→ 0	2
y: ───┼─────┼─────┼────→ 0	3
Diagram 113: Substitution - Rearranging First
EXAMPLE: Need to rearrange first
Solve: 2x + y = 7	(1)
3x - 2y = 4	(2)

STEP 1: Choose equation to rearrange Equation (1) is simpler
(y has coefficient 1)

STEP 2: Express y in terms of x From (1): 2x + y = 7
y = 7 - 2x ... (3)

REARRANGEMENT VISUAL:
2x + y = 7
↓ (-2x)
y = 7 - 2x

STEP 3: Substitute into equation (2) 3x - 2(7 - 2x) = 4

STEP 4: Expand brackets
3x - 14 + 4x = 4

EXPANSION BOX:
-2(7 - 2x)
-2×7 + -2×(-2x)
-14 + 4x

STEP 5: Simplify and solve 7x - 14 = 4
7x = 18
x = 18/7

STEP 6: Find y using equation (3)

y=7 -2(18/7)y=7 -36/7y=(49- 36)/7y=13/7EXACT SOLUTION:
┌──────────────────┐
│ x = 18/7	│
│ y = 13/7	│
└──────────────────┘

DECIMAL FORM:
┌──────────────────┐
│ x ≈ 2.57	│
│ y ≈ 1.86	│
└──────────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Eq 1: 2(18/7) + 13/7 = 36/7 + 13/7 = 49/7 = 7 ✓
Eq 2: 3(18/7) - 2(13/7) = 54/7 - 26/7 = 28/7 = 4 ✓

STEP-BY-STEP PATH:
2x + y = 7
↓
y = 7 - 2x
↓ (substitute) 3x - 2(7-2x) = 4
↓ (expand) 3x - 14 + 4x = 4
↓ (simplify) 7x = 18
↓
x = 18/7
↓ (substitute back) y = 7 - 2(18/7)
↓
y = 13/7

Solving One Linear and One Quadratic
Diagram 114: Linear-Quadratic Systems
SYSTEM TYPE:
┌────────────────────────┐
│ Linear: y = mx + c	│
│ Quadratic: y = ax²+bx+c│
└────────────────────────┘

KEY POINTS:
Can have 0, 1, or 2 solutions
Use substitution method
Leads to quadratic equation

POSSIBLE OUTCOMES:

TWO SOLUTIONS
Line intersects parabola twice

/\
/ \
/	\
/	\

↑	↑
Point 1 Point 2

ONE SOLUTION
Line touches parabola (tangent)

/\
/ \
/	•
/

↑
Touch point

NO SOLUTION
Line misses parabola

/\
/ \
/	\

Line doesn't touch

GRAPHICAL INTERPRETATION:
y
│	╱\
│	╱ \ Parabola
│ ╱	\
│ ╱ ●	\
│╱	│	\
│────●─────\─── x
│ Linear line

Two intersection points = 2 solutions Diagram 115: Solving Linear-Quadratic Example EXAMPLE: Line and parabola
Solve: y = x + 2	... (1) [Linear]
y = x² - 4x + 6 ... (2) [Quadratic]

STEP 1: Substitute (1) into (2) Since both equal y:
x + 2 = x² - 4x + 6

SUBSTITUTION VISUAL:
y = x + 2
y = x² - 4x + 6
↓ (both equal y)
x + 2 = x² - 4x + 6

STEP 2: Rearrange to standard form 0 = x² - 4x + 6 - x - 2
0 = x² - 5x + 4 x² - 5x + 4 = 0

STEP 3: Factorize
Need factors of 4 that add to -5 Factors: -4 and -1
(x - 4)(x - 1) = 0 FACTOR CHECK:
4
/ \
-4 -1
└─┬─┘
-5 (sum)

STEP 4: Solve for x
x - 4 = 0 OR x - 1 = 0 x = 4	OR x = 1

STEP 5: Find corresponding y values For x = 4:
y = 4 + 2 = 6

For x = 1:
y = 1 + 2 = 3

SOLUTIONS:
┌──────────────────────┐
│ Solution 1: (4, 6)	│
│ Solution 2: (1, 3)	│
└──────────────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Solution 1: (4, 6)
Eq 1: y = 4 + 2 = 6 ✓
Eq 2: y = 16 - 16 + 6 = 6 ✓

Solution 2:(1,3)Eq 1: y = 1+ 2= 3 ✓Eq 2: y = 1- 4+ 6 = 3 ✓GRAPHICAL CHECK:
y
│	/\
6 │	/ \
│	/ ● \ (4,6)
5 │ /	\
4 │ /	\
3 │/ ●────────\ (1,3)
2 │/	\
1 │
└──────────────── x 0 1 2 3 4 5

Line y = x + 2 intersects parabola at (1,3) and (4,6)

SOLUTION PATH:
y = x + 2
y = x² - 4x + 6
↓
x + 2 = x² - 4x + 6
↓
x² - 5x + 4 = 0
↓
(x-4)(x-1) = 0
↓
x = 4 or x = 1
↓
(4,6) or (1,3)

Word Problems with Simultaneous Equations
Diagram 116: Problem-Solving Framework
WORD PROBLEM STRATEGY:
┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ 1. READ problem carefully	│
│ 2. IDENTIFY what to find	│
│ 3. DEFINE variables	│
│ 4. TRANSLATE to equations	│
│ 5. SOLVE system	│
│ 6. INTERPRET solution	│
│ 7. CHECK answer makes sense	│
└────────────────────────────────┘ COMMON WORD PROBLEM TYPES:
┌─────────────────────────┐
│ 1. Number Problems	│
│ 2. Age Problems	│
│ 3. Money/Cost Problems │
│ 4. Distance/Speed	│
│ 5. Mixture Problems	│
│ 6. Digit Problems	│
└─────────────────────────┘

TRANSLATION PHRASES:
"sum of" → + "difference" → - "total" → + "more than" → + "less than" → - "twice" → 2×
"half" → ÷2 or ×1/2 "is/equals" → =
Diagram 117: Number Problem Example
PROBLEM: The sum of two numbers is 25 and their difference is 7.
Find the numbers.

STEP 1: Define variables
Let first number = x Let second number = y

STEP 2: Translate to equations "sum is 25"
x + y = 25 ... (1)

"difference is 7" x - y = 7 ... (2)

VISUAL REPRESENTATION:
├──x──┤
├─y── ┤
└──7──┘
├──25──┤

STEP 3: Choose method
Elimination (coefficients of y are equal)

STEP 4: Add equations
x + y = 25 x - y = 7
───────── 2x = 32
x = 16

ADDITION VISUAL:
x + y = 25
+ x - y = 7
─────────── 2x + 0 = 32

STEP 5: Find y
Substitute x = 16 into (1):
16 + y = 25
y = 9

SOLUTION:
┌──────────────────────┐
│ First number = 16	│
│ Second number = 9	│
└──────────────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Sum: 16 + 9 = 25 ✓
Difference: 16 - 9 = 7 ✓
NUMBER LINE:
├────┼─────┼────┼────┼────┤ 0	9	16	25	30
↑	↑
y	x
└──7──┘

Diagram 118: Cost Problem Example
PROBLEM: At a shop, 3 pens and 2 books cost ₦450. 5 pens and 4 books cost ₦830. Find the cost of one pen and one book.

STEP 1: Define variables
Let cost of 1 pen = ₦x Let cost of 1 book = ₦y

VISUAL:
= ₦450
(3 pens) (2 books)

= ₦830
(5 pens)	(4 books)

STEP 2: Form equations
3x + 2y = 450 ... (1)
5x + 4y = 830 ... (2)

STEP 3: Use elimination
Multiply (1) by 2:
6x + 4y = 900 ... (3)

STEP 4: Subtract
(3) - (2):
6x + 4y = 900
-(5x + 4y = 830)
───────────────── x = 70

STEP 5: Find y
Substitute x = 70 into (1):
3(70) + 2y = 450
210 + 2y = 450
2y = 240
y = 120

SOLUTION:
┌────────────────────────┐
│ Cost of 1 pen = ₦70	│
│ Cost of 1 book = ₦120 │
└────────────────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Eq 1: 3(70) + 2(120) = 210 + 240 = 450 ✓
Eq 2: 5(70) + 4(120) = 350 + 480 = 830 ✓

COST TABLE:
┌──────┬────┬──────┬───────┐
│ Item │Qty │Price │ Total │
├──────┼────┼──────┼───────┤
│ Pen │ 3 │ ₦70 │ ₦210	│
│ Book │ 2 │₦120 │ ₦240	│
│	│	│	│ ₦450 ✓│
├──────┼────┼──────┼───────┤
│ Pen │ 5 │ ₦70 │ ₦350	│
│ Book │ 4 │₦120 │ ₦480	│
│	│	│	│ ₦830 ✓│
└──────┴────┴──────┴───────┘

ANSWER: One pen costs ₦70
One book costs ₦120

Diagram 119: Age Problem Example
PROBLEM: A man is 3 times as old as his son. In 12 years, he will be twice as old as his son.
Find their present ages.

STEP 1: Define variables
Let son's present age = x years
Let man's present age = y years

AGE TABLE:
┌────────────┬─────────┬─────────┐
│	│ Now	│In 12 yrs│
├────────────┼─────────┼─────────┤

└────────────┴─────────┴─────────┘ STEP 2: Form equations
"man is 3 times son's age" y = 3x ... (1)

"in 12 years, twice as old" y + 12 = 2(x + 12) ... (2)

VISUAL NOW:
Man's age: ●●●●●●●●● (3 times) Son's age: ●●●	(1 times)

STEP 3: Simplify equation (2) y + 12 = 2x + 24
y = 2x + 12 ... (3)

STEP 4: Use substitution
From (1): y = 3x Substitute into (3): 3x = 2x + 12
x = 12

STEP 5: Find y
y = 3(12) = 36

SOLUTION:
┌───────────────────────┐
│ Son's age = 12 years │
│ Man's age = 36 years │
└───────────────────────┘
VERIFICATION:
Now: Man is 36, Son is 12 36 = 3 × 12 ✓

In 12 years: Man is 48, Son is 24
48 = 2 × 24 ✓

TIMELINE:
Now	In 12 years Son: 12 ────────→ 24
↕×3	↕×2
Man: 36 ────────→ 48

AGE RATIO:
Now: 36:12 = 3:1
Future: 48:24 = 2:1

ANSWER: Son is 12 years old Man is 36 years old
Worked Examples with Complete Solutions:
Example 1: Solve by elimination: 3x + 4y = 25 2x - 3y = -6
Diagram 120: Complete Elimination Solution
Given: 3x + 4y = 25 ... (1) 2x - 3y = -6 ... (2)

STEP 1: Choose variable to eliminate Let's eliminate x

STEP 2: Find LCM of x-coefficients Coefficients: 3 and 2
LCM = 6

MULTIPLICATION STRATEGY:
┌────────────────────────┐
│ Eq (1) × 2 → 6x + 8y	│
│ Eq (2) × 3 → 6x - 9y	│
└────────────────────────┘

STEP 3: Multiply equations
(1) × 2: 6x + 8y = 50 ... (3)
(2) × 3: 6x - 9y = -18 ... (4)

MULTIPLICATION CHECK:
(1) × 2: 3x×2 + 4y×2 = 25×2
6x + 8y = 50 ✓

(2) × 3: 2x×3 - 3y×3 = -6×3 6x - 9y = -18 ✓

STEP 4: Subtract equations (3) - (4):

6x + 8y = 50
-(6x - 9y = -18)
────────────────── 0 + 17y = 68

SUBTRACTION BREAKDOWN:
┌──────────────────────────┐
│ 6x - 6x = 0	│
│ 8y - (-9y) = 8y+9y = 17y │
│ 50 - (-18) = 50+18 = 68 │
└──────────────────────────┘

STEP 5: Solve for y
17y = 68
y = 4

STEP 6: Substitute into original (1)
3x + 4(4) = 25
3x + 16 = 25
3x = 9
x = 3 SOLUTION BOX:
┌──────────────┐
│ x = 3	│
│ y = 4	│
└──────────────┘ VERIFICATION:
Equation1: 3(3) + 4(4)=9+16=25 ✓Equation2: 2(3) - 3(4)=6-12=-6 ✓COMPLETE
3x +
2x -

6x +
6x -SOLUTION PATH: 4y = 25
3y = -6
↓ (×2, ×3)
8y = 50
9y = -18

↓(subtract)17y= 68↓(÷17)y = 4
↓ (substitute) 3x + 16 = 25
↓
x = 3

GRAPHICAL REPRESENTATION:
y
│
5 │
4 │	● (3,4)
3 │	/│\
2 │	/ │ \
1 │	/ │ \
│	/	│	\		x 0	1	2	3	4 5
Lines intersect at (3, 4) ANSWER: x = 3, y = 4
Example 2: Solve by substitution: y = 3x - 5 2x + 3y = 4
Diagram 121: Complete Substitution Solution
Given: y = 3x - 5 ... (1)
2x + 3y = 4 ... (2)

STEP 1: Identify substitution
Equation (1) already solved for y!

STEP 2: Substitute into equation (2) 2x + 3(3x - 5) = 4

SUBSTITUTION DIAGRAM: 2x + 3y = 4
↓
2x + 3(3x-5) = 4
└──────┘
Replace y with 3x-5

STEP 3: Expand brackets
2x + 9x - 15 = 4

EXPANSION VISUAL: 3(3x - 5)
= 3×3x + 3×(-5)
= 9x - 15

STEP 4: Simplify
11x - 15 = 4
11x = 19 x = 19/11
STEP 5: Find y using equation (1) y = 3(19/11) - 5
y = 57/11 - 55/11
y = 2/11 EXACT SOLUTION:
┌──────────────────┐
│ x = 19/11	│
│ y = 2/11	│
└──────────────────┘ DECIMAL APPROXIMATION:
┌──────────────────┐
│ x ≈ 1.73	│
│ y ≈ 0.18	│
└──────────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Eq 1: y = 3(19/11) - 5
= 57/11 - 55/11
= 2/11 ✓

Eq 2: 2(19/11) + 3(2/11)
= 38/11 + 6/11
= 44/11
= 4 ✓

SOLUTION FLOWCHART:
y = 3x - 5 2x + 3y = 4
↓
2x + 3(3x-5) = 4
↓
2x + 9x - 15 = 4
↓
11x = 19
↓
x = 19/11
↓
y = 3(19/11) - 5
↓
y = 2/11

FRACTION WORK:
For y:
3(19/11) - 5
= 57/11 - 5
= 57/11 - 55/11 (LCD = 11)
= (57-55)/11
= 2/11

ANSWER: x = 19/11, y = 2/11
or x ≈ 1.73, y ≈ 0.18 Example 3: Solve: y = x² - 2x + 3 y = 2x + 1 Diagram 122: Linear-Quadratic Solution
Given: y = x² - 2x + 3 ... (1) [Quadratic] y = 2x + 1	... (2) [Linear]

STEP 1: Equate the expressions Since both equal y:
x² - 2x + 3 = 2x + 1

EQUATION VISUAL:
y = x² - 2x + 3 y = 2x + 1
↓ (set equal)
x² - 2x + 3 = 2x + 1

STEP 2: Rearrange to standard form x² - 2x + 3 - 2x - 1 = 0
x² - 4x + 2 = 0

REARRANGEMENT STEPS:
x² - 2x + 3 = 2x + 1 x² - 2x - 2x = 1 - 3 x² - 4x = -2
x² - 4x + 2 = 0

STEP 3: Use quadratic formula a = 1, b = -4, c = 2

x = (-b ± √(b² - 4ac))/2a
x = (4 ± √(16 - 8))/2 x = (4 ± √8)/2
x = (4 ± 2√2)/2
x = 2 ± √2

DISCRIMINANT:
Δ = (-4)² - 4(1)(2)
= 16 - 8
= 8 > 0

Two distinct solutions ✓

STEP 4: Calculate x values
x₁ = 2 + √2 ≈ 2 + 1.414 = 3.414
x₂ = 2 - √2 ≈ 2 - 1.414 = 0.586

STEP 5: Find corresponding y values For x₁ = 2 + √2:
y₁ = 2(2 + √2) + 1
= 4 + 2√2 + 1
= 5 + 2√2
≈ 7.83

For x₂ = 2 - √2: y₂ = 2(2 - √2) + 1
= 4 - 2√2 + 1
= 5 - 2√2
≈ 2.17

EXACT SOLUTIONS:
┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ Solution 1: (2+√2, 5+2√2)	│
│ Solution 2: (2-√2, 5-2√2)	│
└─────────────────────────────┘

APPROXIMATE SOLUTIONS:
┌─────────────────────────────┐
│ Solution 1: (3.41, 7.83)	│
│ Solution 2: (0.59, 2.17)	│
└─────────────────────────────┘

VERIFICATION (using x ≈ 3.41): Eq 1: y = (3.41)² - 2(3.41) + 3
≈ 11.63 - 6.82 + 3
≈ 7.81 ≈ 7.83 ✓

Eq 2: y = 2(3.41) + 1
= 6.82 + 1
= 7.82 ≈ 7.83 ✓
GRAPHICAL VISUALIZATION:
y
│	/\
8 │	/ \
│	/ ● \ (3.41, 7.83)
6 │	/	\
│ / ●──────\ (0.59, 2.17)
4 │ /	\
2 │/	\
└──────────────── x 0 1 2 3 4

Parabola: y = x² - 2x + 3 Line: y = 2x + 1 Intersections at both points

SOLUTION PATH:
y = x² - 2x + 3 y = 2x + 1
↓
x² - 2x + 3 = 2x + 1
↓
x² - 4x + 2 = 0
↓
x = (4 ± √8)/2
↓
x = 2 ± √2
↓
(2+√2, 5+2√2) or (2-√2, 5-2√2)

Example 4: At a fruit stall, 5 oranges and 3 apples cost ₦200. 7 oranges and 5 apples cost ₦310. Find the cost of each fruit.
Diagram 123: Cost Problem Complete Solution
Given Information:
5 oranges + 3 apples = ₦200
7 oranges + 5 apples = ₦310

VISUAL REPRESENTATION:
Scenario 1:
����� ��� = ₦200

Scenario 2:
������� �����  = ₦310

STEP 1: Define variables
Let cost of 1 orange = ₦x Let cost of 1 apple = ₦y

STEP 2: Form equations
5x + 3y = 200 ... (1)
7x + 5y = 310 ... (2)

STEP 3: Use elimination method Choose to eliminate x LCM of 5 and 7 = 35

MULTIPLICATION PLAN:
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ Eq (1) × 7 → 35x + 21y	│
│ Eq (2) × 5 → 35x + 25y	│
└────────────────────────────┘

STEP 4: Multiplyequations(1) × 7:35x + 21y = 1400... (3)(2) × 5:35x + 25y = 1550... (4)STEP 5: Subtract(4)-(3):
35x + 25y = 1550
-(35x + 21y = 1400)
──────────────────── 0 + 4y = 150
SUBTRACTION DETAIL:
┌──────────────────────────┐
│ 35x - 35x = 0	│
│ 25y - 21y = 4y	│
│ 1550 - 1400 = 150	│
└──────────────────────────┘

STEP 6: Solve for y
4y = 150
y = 37.5

STEP 7: Substitute into (1)
5x + 3(37.5) = 200
5x + 112.5 = 200
5x = 87.5
x = 17.5

SOLUTION:
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ Cost of 1 orange = ₦17.50  │
│ Cost of 1 apple = ₦37.50	│
└────────────────────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Scenario 1:
5(17.5) + 3(37.5)
= 87.5 + 112.5
= 200 ✓

Scenario 2:
7(17.5) + 5(37.5)
= 122.5 + 187.5
= 310 ✓

COST BREAKDOWN TABLE:
┌────────┬────┬────────┬────────┐
│ Fruit │Qty │ Price │ Total │
├────────┼────┼────────┼────────┤
│ Orange │ 5 │ ₦17.50│ ₦87.50 │
│ Apple  │ 3  │ ₦37.50│₦112.50  │
│	│	│	│ ₦200 ✓ │
├────────┼────┼────────┼────────┤
│ Orange │ 7  │ ₦17.50│₦122.50  │
│ Apple  │ 5  │ ₦37.50│₦187.50  │
│	│	│	│ ₦310 ✓ │
└────────┴────┴────────┴────────┘ PRICE COMPARISON:
Apple is more expensive:
₦37.50 - ₦17.50 = ₦20 more

Ratio: 37.5:17.5 ≈ 2.14:1
(Apples cost about twice as much)

ANSWER:
One orange costs ₦17.50 One apple costs ₦37.50
Class Activity:
Activity 1: Method Comparison Diagram 124: Solve Using Both Methods
Solve using BOTH elimination and substitution: x + y = 10
x - y = 2

METHOD 1: ELIMINATION
x + y = 10
+ x - y = 2
────────── 2x = 12
x = 6 Then: y = 4
METHOD 2: SUBSTITUTION
From (1): y = 10 - x Into (2): x - (10-x) = 2
2x = 12
x = 6 Then: y = 4

Same answer! ✓

Shopping (costs of items)

Age relationships
Mixture problems

y = x + 1 and y = 3 - x
2x + y = 6 and x - y = 3
Find solutions graphically, then verify algebraically.

Three books and two pens cost ₦350. Five books and three pens cost ₦550. Find the cost of each.
The sum of two numbers is 50. If the larger number is 10 more than the smaller, find both numbers.

Assignment:

2.	Solve by substitution: (a) x = 3y - 2, 2x + y = 11 (b) y = 2x + 1, 3x - 4y = -13 3.	Solve: (a) y = x² - 4x + 5, y = 2x - 1 (b) y = x² + 2x + 1, y = x + 3
At a restaurant, 4 meals and 3 drinks cost ₦2,400. 6 meals and 5 drinks cost ₦3,700. Find the cost of one meal and one drink.
The perimeter of a rectangle is 40 cm. The length is 4 cm more than the width. Find the dimensions.

WEEK 10: GRAPHICAL SOLUTIONS
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
Draw graphs of linear equations
Draw graphs of quadratic equations (parabolas)
Interpret graphs of equations
Solve simultaneous equations graphically
Find solutions from intersection points
Compare graphical and algebraic solutions
Apply graphical methods to real-life problems
Entry Behaviour:
Students can plot points on coordinate axes, understand linear and quadratic equations, and solve equations algebraically.
Instructional Materials:
Graph paper
Rulers and pencils
Set squares
Colored pencils/markers
Coordinate grid charts
Calculator
Transparencies for overlays
Content:
Coordinate System Review Diagram 125: The Cartesian Plane THE COORDINATE SYSTEM
y-axis (vertical)
↑
│
│	I
│
──────┼──────→ x-axis (horizontal)
│
│ IV
│
↓

QUADRANTS:
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ I:	x > 0, y > 0 (+,+)	│
│ II: x < 0, y > 0 (-,+)	│
│ III: x < 0, y < 0 (-,-)	│
│ IV: x > 0, y < 0 (+,-)	│
└────────────────────────────┘

PLOTTING POINTS:
Point (3, 2):
Start at origin (0, 0)
Move 3 units right (x = 3)
Move 2 units up (y = 2)
Mark the point

VISUAL:
y
3 │
2 │	● (3,2)
1 │
├──────┼──────→ x 0 1 2 3 4

KEY FEATURES:
Origin: (0, 0)
x-intercept: where line crosses x-axis (y = 0)
y-intercept: where line crosses y-axis (x = 0)

SCALE:
Important to choose appropriate scale!

Small values: 1 square = 1 unit
Large values: 1 square = 2, 5, or 10 units

EXAMPLE SCALES:
1 cm = 1 unit (for values 0-20)
1 cm = 2 units (for values 0-40)
1 cm = 5 units (for values 0-100)

Graphing Linear Equations
Diagram 126: Methods for Drawing Linear Graphs
LINEAR EQUATION FORM:
y = mx + c

Where:
m = gradient (slope) c = y-intercept

METHOD 1: TABLE OF VALUES
┌──────────────────────────┐
│ 1. Choose x values	│
│ 2. Calculate y values	│
│ 3. Create table	│
│ 4. Plot points	│
│ 5. Draw straight line	│
└──────────────────────────┘ EXAMPLE: y = 2x + 1
TABLE:
┌────┬────┬────┬────┬────┐
│ x │ -2 │ -1 │ 0 │ 1 │ 2 │
├────┼────┼────┼────┼────┤
│ y │ -3 │ -1 │ 1 │ 3 │ 5 │
└────┴────┴────┴────┴────┘

Calculations:
x = -2: y = 2(-2) + 1 = -3
x = -1: y = 2(-1) + 1 = -1
x = 0: y = 2(0) + 1 = 1
x = 1: y = 2(1) + 1 = 3
x = 2: y = 2(2) + 1 = 5 GRAPH:
y
5 │	●
4 │	/
3 │	●
2 │	/
1 │	●
0 ├─●───────────→ x
-1 │ ●
-2 │/
-3 ●
────────────────────────────────── METHOD 2: INTERCEPT METHOD
(Faster for equations in form ax + by = c)

Steps:
Find y-intercept (set x = 0)
Find x-intercept (set y = 0)
Plot both points
Draw line through them EXAMPLE: 2x + 3y = 6
y-intercept (x = 0): 3y = 6
y = 2
Point: (0, 2)

x-intercept (y = 0): 2x = 6
x = 3
Point: (3, 0)

GRAPH:
y
3 │
2 ●───────
1 │ \
0 ├───\────●───→ x
0	1	2	3
────────────────────────────────── METHOD 3: GRADIENT-INTERCEPT
(For equations in form y = mx + c)

Steps:
Plot y-intercept (0, c)
Use gradient m = rise/run
From y-intercept, count:
run (horizontal)
rise (vertical)
Mark second point
Draw line

EXAMPLE: y = (3/2)x - 1

y-intercept: c = -1 Point: (0, -1)

Gradient: m = 3/2 = rise/run From (0, -1):
Run 2 right
Rise 3 up
New point: (2, 2)

GRADIENT VISUAL:
(2, 2)
/│
/ │ rise = 3
/ │
───┘ (0, -1)
run = 2

CHARACTERISTICS:
┌──────────────────────────┐
│ Positive gradient /	│
│ (line slopes upward)	│
│	│
│ Negative gradient \	│
│ (line slopes downward)	│
│	│
│ Zero gradient ──	│
│ (horizontal line)	│
│	│
│ Undefined gradient │	│
│ (vertical line)	│
└──────────────────────────┘

Graphing Quadratic Equations
Diagram 128: Parabola Basics
QUADRATIC FORM:
y = ax² + bx + c

PARABOLA SHAPE:
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ a > 0: Opens upward ∪	│
│ a < 0: Opens downward ∩	│
└────────────────────────────┘
UPWARD (a > 0):
y
│	/\
│ / \
│ /	\
│/	\
└────────→ x
Minimum point at vertex DOWNWARD (a < 0):
y
│\	/
│ \	/
│ \ /
│	\/
└────────→ x
Maximum point at vertex KEY FEATURES:
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ • Vertex (turning point)	│
│ • Axis of symmetry	│
│ • y-intercept (c)	│
│ • x-intercepts (roots)	│
│ • Domain: all real x	│
│ • Range: depends on vertex │
└────────────────────────────┘

VERTEX FORMULA:
x-coordinate of vertex = -b/(2a) Then substitute to find y
AXIS OF SYMMETRY:
Vertical line through vertex x = -b/(2a)

y
│	/│\
│ / │ \
│ / │ \
│/	│	\
└────┼────→ x axis
Diagram 129: Drawing Parabolas - Step by Step
EXAMPLE: Draw y = x² - 4x + 3

STEP 1: Create table of values Choose x values around vertex

x-coordinate of vertex:
x = -b/(2a) = -(-4)/(2×1) = 2
Choose x from 0 to 4: TABLE:
┌───┬───┬───┬───┬───┬───┐
│ x │ 0 │ 1 │ 2 │ 3 │ 4 │
├───┼───┼───┼───┼───┼───┤
│ y │ 3 │ 0 │-1 │ 0 │ 3 │
└───┴───┴───┴───┴───┴───┘

CALCULATIONS:
x = 0: y = 0 - 0 + 3 = 3
x = 1: y = 1 - 4 + 3 = 0
x = 2: y = 4 - 8 + 3 = -1
x = 3: y = 9 - 12 + 3 = 0
x = 4: y = 16 - 16 + 3 = 3
STEP 2: Plot points y
4 │
3 ●	●
2 │
1 │
0 ├─●───────●───→ x
-1 │	●

Points: (0,3), (1,0), (2,-1), (3,0), (4,3)

STEP 3: Draw smooth curve y
4 │
3 ●─────────●
2 │ \	/
1 │	\ /
0 ├─●──●──●───→ x
-1 │	V
0 1 2 3 4

FEATURES:
Vertex: (2, -1) ← minimum
Axis of symmetry: x = 2
y-intercept: (0, 3)
x-intercepts: (1, 0) and (3, 0) [These are the roots!]

SYMMETRY CHECK:
Points equidistant from x = 2 have same y-value:
(0, 3) and (4, 3) ✓
(1, 0) and (3, 0) ✓
────────────────────────────────── TIPS FOR ACCURATE DRAWING:
Use at least 5-7 points
Include vertex
Mark intercepts clearly
Use smooth curve (not straight lines)
Check symmetry
Extend curve slightly beyond table values
Use pencil for corrections

COMMON MISTAKES:
✗ Connecting points with straight lines
✗ Not enough points
✗ Asymmetric curve
✗ Wrong shape (opening direction)
Solving Simultaneous Equations Graphically Diagram 130: Graphical Solution Method PRINCIPLE:
Solution = Intersection point(s)

TWO LINEAR EQUATIONS:
Usually ONE intersection point

EXAMPLE:
y = 2x + 1 ... (1)
y = -x + 4 ... (2)

↑

────────────────────────────────── LINEAR + QUADRATIC:
Can have 0, 1, or 2 solutions

EXAMPLE:
y = x² - 2x - 3 (parabola) y = x + 1	(line)

GRAPH:
y
6 │	/\
4 │	/ \
2 │	/ ●──●──
0 ├───●────●───→ x
-2 │ /	\
-4 │ V

Two intersections: Solution 1: (-1, 0)
Solution 2: (4, 5)

VERIFICATION:
For (-1, 0):
Parabola: 1 + 2 - 3 = 0 ✓
Line: -1 + 1 = 0 ✓

For (4, 5):
Parabola: 16 - 8 - 3 = 5 ✓
Line: 4 + 1 = 5 ✓

────────────────────────────────── READING SOLUTIONS:
ACCURATE READING:
Use sharp pencil
Read to nearest 0.1 or 0.5
Check scale carefully
Verify algebraically

GRID INTERSECTION:
If intersection falls on grid point: Solution is exact

If between grid points: Solution is approximate

EXAMPLE:
│
──X── Intersection at (2.5, 3)
│
2	3

Approximate: x = 2.5, y = 3

Diagram 131: Special Cases
CASE 1: PARALLEL LINES
No intersection = No solution

y
│ Line 1 (y = 2x + 3)
│ /
│/
─────────→ x
/
/ Line 2 (y = 2x - 1)

Same gradient, different intercepts Lines never meet!
────────────────────────────────── CASE 2: COINCIDENT LINES
Same line = Infinite solutions

y
│ /
│/ Both lines overlap
═══════════→ x

Every point on line is a solution!
────────────────────────────────── CASE 3: LINE MISSES PARABOLA
No intersection = No real solutions

y
│
│ /	\ Parabola
│/	\
───────────→ x Line below

Line and parabola don't meet (System has complex solutions)
────────────────────────────────── CASE 4: LINE TANGENT TO PARABOLA
One touch point = One solution

y
│	/\
│ / \
│ /	•	→ x
│	\

Line touches parabola at exactly one point (tangent)

INTERCEPTS

GRADIENT (SLOPE)
┌────────────────────────────┐
│ Steeper line = larger m	│
│ Positive m: /	│
│ Negative m: \	│
│ Zero m: ──	│
└────────────────────────────┘
CALCULATING FROM GRAPH:
Pick two points: (x₁, y₁) and (x₂, y₂)

m = (y₂ - y₁)/(x₂ - x₁)
= rise/run

EXAMPLE:
Points: (0, 1) and (2, 5)

m = (5 - 1)/(2 - 0)
= 4/2
= 2

Example 2: Draw the graph of y = x² - 4x + 3 for 0 ≤ x ≤ 4
Diagram 134: Complete Parabola Solution
Given: y = x² - 4x + 3 Domain: 0 ≤ x ≤ 4

STEP 1: Find vertex
x-coordinate: x = -b/(2a)
= -(-4)/(2×1)
= 4/2
= 2

y-coordinate: y = (2)² - 4(2) + 3
= 4 - 8 + 3
= -1

Vertex: (2, -1)

STEP 2: Create table
Include vertex and points on each side

└────┴────┴────┴────┴────┴────┘

CALCULATIONS:
x = 0: y = 0 - 0 + 3 = 3
x = 1: y = 1 - 4 + 3 = 0
x = 2: y = 4 - 8 + 3 = -1
x = 3: y = 9 - 12 + 3 = 0
x = 4: y = 16 - 16 + 3 = 3

y < 0 when 1 < x < 3
y = 0 when x = 1 or x = 3

Therefore:

STEP 5: Verify algebraically Equation 1: y = 2(2) + 1 = 5 ✓
Equation 2: y = -(2) + 7 = 5 ✓

ALTERNATIVE VERIFICATION:
Substitute into originals:
Point (2, 5):
5 = 2(2) + 1 → 5 = 5 ✓
5 = -2 + 7 → 5 = 5 ✓

GRADIENT ANALYSIS:
Line 1: m = 2 (positive, upward) Line 2: m = -1 (negative, downward)
Different gradients → one intersection ✓

ANSWER: Solution is x = 2, y = 5 or point (2, 5)
COMPARISON WITH ALGEBRA:
Substitution method:
2x + 1 = -x + 7
3x = 6
x = 2, y = 5 ✓
Same answer!

Parabola opens upward

Solutions are exact (on grid points)

x = -1, y = 0 OR x = 3, y = 4

Example 5: A taxi charges a fixed fee of ₦200 plus ₦50 per km. A second taxi charges ₦100 plus ₦75 per km. Draw graphs and find when costs are equal.

INTERPRETATION:
┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ When distance = 4 km	│
│ Both taxis cost ₦400	│
│	│

│ For d > 4 km: Taxi A cheaper	│
│ For d = 4 km: Same cost	│
└────────────────────────────────┘

VERIFICATION:
Taxi A at 4 km: 50(4) + 200 = 400 ✓
Taxi B at 4 km: 75(4) + 100 = 400 ✓

ALGEBRAIC SOLUTION:
50d + 200 = 75d + 100
200 - 100 = 75d - 50d
100 = 25d
d = 4 km ✓

DECISION TABLE:
┌──────┬────────┬────────┬────────┐
│ d	│ Taxi A │ Taxi B │ Cheaper│
├──────┼────────┼────────┼────────┤

└──────┴────────┴────────┴────────┘ PRACTICAL ADVICE:
Short trips (< 4 km): Choose Taxi B
Long trips (> 4 km): Choose Taxi A
Exactly 4 km: Either taxi

ANSWER:
Costs are equal at 4 km (₦400)

Diagram 140: Essential Formulas Summary
KEY FORMULAS TO REMEMBER:

┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ STANDARD FORM	│
│ A × 10ⁿ (where 1 ≤ A < 10)	│
└────────────────────────────────┘
┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ PERCENTAGE ERROR	│
│ % Error = |Approx-Exact| × 100 │
│	─────────────	│
│	Exact	│
└────────────────────────────────┘

┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ LOGARITHMS	│
│ log(M × N) = log M + log N	│
│ log(M ÷ N) = log M - log N	│
│ log(Mⁿ) = n log M	│
└────────────────────────────────┘

┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ ARITHMETIC PROGRESSION	│
│ Tₙ	= a + (n-1)d	│
│ Sₙ	= n/2[2a + (n-1)d]	│
│ or Sₙ	= n/2(a + l)	│
└────────────────────────────────┘

┌────────────────────────────────┐
│ QUADRATIC EQUATIONS	│
│ x = -b ± √(b²-4ac)	│
│	────────────	│
│	2a	│
│	│
│ Sum of roots: α+β = -b/a	│
│ Product: αβ = c/a	│
│ Discriminant: Δ = b²-4ac	│
└────────────────────────────────┘

Comprehensive Revision Questions
Mixed Topic Problems:
Problem 1: Combining Multiple Topics Diagram 141: Multi-Concept Problem
QUESTION:
The roots of the equation x² - px + 12 = 0
are in arithmetic progression with common difference 1. Find p and solve the equation.

SOLUTION:

STEP 1: Set up using AP concept Let roots be (a-d) and (a+d)
Given: common difference = 1, so d = 1 Roots: (a-1) and (a+1)

STEP 2: Use product of roots Product = c/a = 12/1 = 12
(a-1)(a+1) = 12
a² - 1 = 12
a² = 13
a = √13 (taking positive)

STEP 3: Find p using sum Sum = -b/a = p/1 = p
(a-1) + (a+1) = p
2a = p
p = 2√13

STEP 4: Write equation x² - 2√13x + 12 = 0

STEP 5: Roots are x = √13 ± 1

VERIFICATION:
Sum: (√13-1) + (√13+1) = 2√13 ✓
Product: (√13-1)(√13+1) = 13-1 = 12 ✓

Topics Used:
Quadratic equations
Sum and product of roots
Arithmetic progression
Surds
Problem 2: Real-World Application
Diagram 142: Practical Problem
QUESTION:
A number in standard form is 3.5 × 10⁴. If it's increased by 20%, express the new number in standard form correct to
3 significant figures.

SOLUTION:

STEP 1: Find actual number 3.5 × 10⁴ = 35,000

STEP 2: Calculate 20% increase
20% of 35,000 = 0.2 × 35,000
= 7,000

New number = 35,000 + 7,000
= 42,000

STEP 3: Express in standard form 42,000 = 4.2 × 10⁴

STEP 4: Check significant figures
4.2 already has 2 s.f. To 3 s.f.: 4.20 × 10⁴

ANSWER: 4.20 × 10⁴

Topics Used:
Standard form
Percentages
Significant figures Problem 3: Sequential Problem Diagram 143: Multi-Step Question
QUESTION:
Find the 10th term of the AP: 3, 7, 11, ... Then use this as the constant term to
form a quadratic equation with roots
2 and 5. Solve it graphically.

SOLUTION:

PART A: Find 10th term a = 3, d = 4
T₁₀ = 3 + (10-1)×4
= 3 + 36
= 39

PART B: Form equation Roots: 2 and 5
Sum: 2 + 5 = 7
Product: 2 × 5 = 10

But we want constant = 39
So multiply: (x-2)(x-5) × 3.9 Or use: x² - 7x + 39 (adjusted)

Actually, standard form:
(x-2)(x-5) = 0 x² - 7x + 10 = 0

If constant should be 39:
Need different roots or approach

Let's use 39 as y-intercept: y = x² - 7x + 39
PART C: Graph (would show parabola) Topics Used:
Arithmetic progression
Quadratic equations
Graphing
Problem interpretation

= 1.8 minutes per mark

mark question → 9 minutes 10-mark question → 18 minutes

Comprehensive Practice Questions:
Section A: Standard Form and Approximation
Express 0.00567 in standard form.

Round 3.14159 to 4 significant figures.

Calculate percentage error when π is approximated as 3.14.
Section B: Logarithms
Use logarithm tables to find log 345.

Evaluate using logarithms: 45.6	2.3 ÷ 8.4
Find the value of (3.5)⁴	using logarithms.

Section C: Sequences
7.	Find the 25th term of: 5, 9, 13, 17, ...

Calculate the sum of first 20 terms of: 3, 7, 11, 15, ...

The 5th term is 18 and 12th term is 46. Find first term and common difference.

Section D: Quadratic Equations
Solve by factorization: x² - 7x + 12 = 0
Solve by completing square: x² + 6x - 7 = 0
Use quadratic formula: 2x² - 5x - 3 = 0
Form equation with roots 4 and -2.

If one root of x² + kx - 6 = 0 is 3, find k and other root.
Section E: Simultaneous Equations
Solve by elimination: 3x + 2y = 13 2x - y = 4

Solve by substitution: y = 2x - 1 3x + y = 14

Solve: y = x² - 2x y = x + 4
Section F: Graphical Solutions
Draw y = 2x - 3 for -1 ≤ x ≤ 3.

Draw y = x² - 4x + 3 for 0 ≤ x ≤ 4. Find vertex.
Solve graphically: y = x + 1 y = 5 - x

Question 1 (10 marks): (a) Solve by factorization: x² - 8x + 15 = 0 (b) Verify your answer
Question 2 (12 marks): The 3rd term of an AP is 14 and the 9th term is 38. (a) Find the first term and common difference (b) Find the 20th term (c) Calculate the sum of first 15 terms
Question 3 (15 marks): Solve the simultaneous equations: 3x + 2y = 16 5x - y = 9 (a) By elimination method (b) By substitution method (c) Verify both give same answer
Question 4 (13 marks): Draw the graphs of: y = x² - 2x - 3 y = x + 1 for -2 ≤ x ≤ 4 Use your graph to solve x² - 2x - 3 = x + 1
Question 5 (10 marks): A shop sells pens at ₦50 each and books at ₦120 each. (a) Write equation for total cost of x pens and y books (b) If total cost is ₦680 and 4 pens were bought, find number of books (c) If 5 items were bought for ₦480, find how many of each
Question 6 (10 marks): Use logarithm tables to evaluate: (34.5 × 2.8) ÷ √156

Form two equations (b) Find the dimensions (c) Calculate the area

SS2 MATHEMATICS LESSON NOTES
SECOND TERM

WEEK 1: STRAIGHT LINE GRAPHS
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
Write and identify equations of straight lines in different forms
Determine x-intercept and y-intercept from equations and graphs
Calculate the gradient (slope) of a straight line
Draw tangents to curves at given points
Apply straight line concepts to solve practical problems
Entry Behaviour:
Students can plot points on the Cartesian plane, draw graphs of linear equations, and understand basic coordinate geometry.
Instructional Materials:
Graph papers
Ruler and pencil
Set squares
Whiteboard and markers
Calculator
Charts showing different forms of linear equations
Content:
Equation of a Straight Line
General forms:
Gradient-intercept form: y = mx + c

m = gradient (slope)
c = y-intercept (where line crosses y-axis)
Standard form: ax + by + c = 0
3.	Two-point form: (y - y₁ )/(y₂	- y₁ ) = (x - x₁ )/(x₂	- x₁ )
Point-gradient form: y - y₁	= m(x - x₁  )
Intercept form: x/a + y/b = 1

a = x-intercept
b = y-intercept

Determination of Intercepts
intercept: The point where the line crosses the x-axis (y = 0)
To find: Set y = 0 and solve for x
Coordinates: (x, 0)
Y-intercept: The point where the line crosses the y-axis (x = 0)
To find: Set x = 0 and solve for y
Coordinates: (0, y)

The line crosses:
Y-axis at (0, 6) → y-intercept = 6
X-axis at (3, 0) → x-intercept = 3
Gradient of a Straight Line
The gradient (slope) measures the steepness of a line. Definition: Rate of change of y with respect to x Formula: m = (y₂ - y₁)/(x₂ - x₁) = rise/run = Δy/Δx Where (x₁, y₁) and (x₂, y₂) are any two points on the line.

 	+	|	|		x 0	2		6

Gradient m = Rise/Run = 5/4 = 1.25

From equation y = mx + c:
The gradient is the coefficient of x, which is m
Properties of gradients: Diagram 4: Types of Gradients

Positive gradient: line slopes upward from left to right
Negative gradient: line slopes downward from left to right
Zero gradient: horizontal line (y = constant)
Undefined gradient: vertical line (x = constant)
Parallel lines have equal gradients (m₁ = m₂)
Perpendicular lines: m₁ × m₂ = -1 (or m₂ = -1/m₁)

Drawing Tangent to a Curve at a Point
A tangent is a straight line that touches a curve at exactly one point without crossing it.
Diagram 6: Tangent to a Curve

Point of tangency: P(2, 4)
Tangent touches curve at exactly one point

Method for drawing tangent:
Locate the point of contact on the curve
Place a ruler so it just touches the curve at that point
Adjust the ruler until the line appears to touch without cutting through
Draw the tangent line
The tangent should be perpendicular to the radius at that point (for circles)

Gradient of tangent: The gradient of the tangent at a point gives the instantaneous rate of change at that point (introduction to differentiation concept).
For a curve: The gradient of the tangent varies from point to point.
Applications
Straight line graphs are used in:
Economics (cost, revenue, demand curves)
Physics (motion graphs, Hooke's law)
Business (break-even analysis)
Geography (temperature variations)
Engineering (stress-strain relationships)
Worked Examples:
Example 1: Find the gradient and y-intercept of the line 3x + 2y = 12.
Solution: Rearrange to y = mx + c form: 2y = -3x + 12 y = -3x/2 + 6 y = -1.5x + 6 Gradient (m) = -1.5 Y-intercept (c) = 6

Example 2: Find the x-intercept and y-intercept of the line 4x - 5y = 20.

For y-intercept (set x = 0): 4(0) - 5y = 20 -5y = 20 y = -4 Y-intercept: (0, -4)

Example 5: Find the equation of the line passing through points (1, 4) and (5, 12).
Solution: First find gradient: m = (12 - 4)/(5 - 1) = 8/4 = 2
Using y - y₁ = m(x - x₁) with point (1, 4): y - 4 = 2(x - 1) y - 4 = 2x - 2 y = 2x + 2

Example 6: Show that the line 2x + 3y = 15 and the line 3x - 2y = 10 are perpendicular.
Solution: For 2x + 3y = 15: 3y = -2x + 15 y = -2x/3 + 5 m₁ = -2/3 For 3x - 2y = 10: -2y = -3x + 10 y = 3x/2 - 5 m₂ = 3/2
Check: m₁ × m₂ = (-2/3) × (3/2) = -6/6 = -1 ✓
Since the product of gradients = -1, the lines are perpendicular.

Example 7: A line passes through (3, 2) and has gradient -1. Find where it crosses the y-axis.
Solution: Using y - y₁ = m(x - x₁): y - 2 = -1(x - 3) y - 2 = -x + 3 y = -x + 5 The y-intercept is 5, so the line crosses the y-axis at (0, 5).
Example 8: Draw the tangent to the curve y = x² at the point (2, 4) and estimate its gradient.
Solution: [Instructions for drawing]
Plot the curve y = x² for x values from -1 to 4
Mark point P(2, 4) on the curve
Draw a tangent at P by placing ruler to just touch at that point
Extend the tangent line
Choose two convenient points on the tangent and calculate gradient

Find the gradient and y-intercept of the line 2x - 4y = 8.

Determine the x-intercept and y-intercept of 3x + 5y = 15.

Find the equation of the line passing through points (-2, 3) and (4, 15).

A line has gradient 4 and passes through point (1, 7). Where does it cross: (a) The y-axis? (b) The x-axis?

WEEK 2: CIRCLE GEOMETRY I
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
State and apply properties of tangents to circles
Prove that tangent is perpendicular to radius at point of contact
Apply the angle in a semicircle theorem
Solve problems involving angles in alternate segments
Apply circle theorems to solve geometric problems
Entry Behaviour:
Students understand basic properties of circles (radius, diameter, circumference, chord) and can measure angles.
Instructional Materials:
Compass and ruler
Protractor
Set squares
Circle theorem charts
String or rope (to demonstrate properties)
Whiteboard and markers
Prepared circle diagrams
Content:
Basic Circle Terminology
Diagram 15: Parts of a Circle

Key Terms:
Center (O): The fixed point equidistant from all points on circle
Radius: Distance from center to any point on circle
Diameter: Line through center joining two points (= 2 × radius)
Chord: Line joining any two points on the circle
Arc: Part of the circumference
Sector: Region between two radii and an arc
Tangent: Line that touches circle at exactly one point
Secant: Line that cuts circle at two points

Tangent Properties
A tangent to a circle is a straight line that touches the circle at exactly one point called the point of contact or point of tangency.
Key Properties:
Property 1: A tangent to a circle is perpendicular to the radius at the point of contact.

Angle between tangent and radius = 90°

Property 2: Two tangents drawn from an external point to a circle are equal in length.

Property 3: The angle between a tangent and a chord through the point of contact equals the angle in the alternate segment.
Perpendicularity of Tangent and Radius
Theorem: The tangent at any point on a circle is perpendicular to the radius through that point.

Practical Application:
At the point where a wheel touches the ground, the radius is vertical and the ground (tangent) is horizontal - they are perpendicular.
Angle in a Semicircle
Theorem: The angle in a semicircle is a right angle (90°).
Any angle subtended by a diameter at the circumference is 90°.

Proof Outline:
Let O be center, A and B endpoints of diameter
Let P be any point on circle
OA = OB = OP = radius
Triangles OAP and OBP are isosceles
Using angle properties, ∠APB = 90°
Angles in Alternate Segments
Theorem (Alternate Segment Theorem): The angle between a tangent and a chord through the point of contact is equal to the angle in the alternate segment.

This confirms the given information. For the chord problem, we would need additional information about the chord's position.

Solution: Since PA is tangent, ∠OAP = 90°
In right triangle OAP: OP² = OA² + PA² (Pythagoras) 13² = r² + 12² 169 = r² + 144 r² = 25 r = 5 cm
Example 3: In a circle with center O, AB is a diameter and C is a point on the circle. If ∠CAB

Solution: Since AB is a diameter: ∠ACB = 90° (angle in semicircle)
In triangle ABC: ∠CAB + ∠CBA + ∠ACB = 180° (sum of angles in triangle) 35° + ∠CBA + 90° = 180° ∠CBA = 180° - 125° ∠CBA = 55°

Solution: Since XY is tangent at P: ∠OPX = 90° (where O is center)
Also, OP bisects ∠QPR (property of tangent) ∠OPQ = ∠OPR = 75°/2 = 37.5°
But we need more information about the position of Q to solve completely. If Q is positioned such that PQ is a chord: ∠QPY = 90° - 37.5° = 52.5°
Class Activity:
Activity 1: Using a compass, draw a circle and construct a tangent at any point on it. Verify that the tangent is perpendicular to the radius.
Activity 2: Draw two tangents from an external point to a circle and measure their lengths to verify they are equal.
Activity 3: Draw a semicircle and mark several points on it. Measure the angles formed at each point with the diameter as base.
Evaluation Questions:
State the relationship between a tangent to a circle and the radius at the point of contact.

In a circle, two tangents from an external point P are each 15 cm long. If the radius is 9 cm, find the distance from P to the center.
AB is a diameter of a circle. If point C is on the circle and ∠BAC = 42°, find ∠BCA and ∠ABC.
A tangent to a circle makes an angle of 55° with a chord drawn from the point of contact. Find the angle subtended by the chord in the alternate segment.
Draw a diagram showing a tangent to a circle and label: center, radius, point of contact, and the perpendicular angle.
Assignment:
Two tangents TA and TB are drawn to a circle from external point T. If TA = 24 cm and the radius is 7 cm, calculate: (a) The distance from T to the center O (b) ∠ATB (if ∠AOB = 120°)
In a circle with center O, diameter PQ = 20 cm. Point R is on the circle such that ∠PRQ = 90°. If PR = 12 cm, find RQ.
A chord AB of a circle makes an angle of 65° with the tangent at A. Calculate: (a) The angle subtended by AB in the alternate segment (b) The angle at the center if the angle at circumference is half of it
Draw a circle of radius 4 cm. From a point 10 cm from the center, draw tangents to the circle. Measure and verify the lengths of the tangents.
Prove using a diagram that the angle in a semicircle is 90°.

WEEK 3: CIRCLE GEOMETRY II
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
State and apply properties of chords
Relate angles at the center to angles at the circumference
Apply properties of cyclic quadrilaterals
Solve problems involving equal chords
Use circle theorems to solve complex geometric problems
Entry Behaviour:
Students understand basic circle properties, angles, and can apply the Pythagorean theorem.
Instructional Materials:
Compass and ruler
Protractor
Circle theorem charts
String or wire for demonstration
Whiteboard and markers
Prepared circle diagrams
Calculator
Content:
Chord Properties
A chord is a straight line joining any two points on the circumference of a circle. The diameter is the longest chord.
Key Properties of Chords:
Property 1: Equal chords are equidistant from the center.

If AB = CD, then OM = ON (d₁ = d₂)

Property 2: The perpendicular from the center of a circle to a chord bisects the chord.

Angle at Center and Angle at Circumference
Major Theorem: The angle subtended by an arc at the center of a circle is twice the angle subtended by the same arc at any point on the remaining part of the circumference.
Formula: Angle at center = 2 × Angle at circumference

Cyclic Quadrilaterals
A cyclic quadrilateral is a four-sided figure whose vertices all lie on the circumference of a circle.
Key Properties:
Property 1: Opposite angles of a cyclic quadrilateral are supplementary (add up to 180°).

Diagram 34: Cyclic Quadrilateral

ABCD is cyclic quadrilateral
∠A + ∠C = 180° (a + c = 180°)
∠B + ∠D = 180° (b + d = 180°)

Property 2: An exterior angle of a cyclic quadrilateral equals the interior opposite angle.

Property 3: If a side of a cyclic quadrilateral is produced, the exterior angle equals the interior opposite angle.
Properties of Equal Chords
Theorem: Equal chords of a circle subtend equal angles at the center and are equidistant from the center.
Diagram 36: Multiple Properties of Equal Chords

Converse: Chords equidistant from the center of a circle are equal in length.
Worked Examples:
Example 1: In a circle with center O, chord AB = 16 cm and its distance from the center is 6 cm. Find the radius of the circle.

Diagram 37:

Solution: Let M be the midpoint of AB where OM ⊥ AB AM = AB/2 = 16/2 = 8 cm (perpendicular from center bisects chord) OM = 6 cm (given)
In right triangle OAM: OA² = OM² + AM² (Pythagoras) r² = 6² + 8² r² = 36 + 64 r² = 100 r = 10 cm

Solution: In a cyclic quadrilateral, opposite angles are supplementary.

Example 4: Two equal chords AB and CD of a circle with center O and radius 13 cm are 5 cm apart. Find the length of each chord.
Diagram 40:
A	C
•	•
|\	/|

|	•M 5cm |
| / O\ • |N
| /	\	|
|/	\ |
B•	•D

OM ⊥ AB, ON ⊥ CD
Distance between chords = 5 cm

Solution: Since chords are equal and equidistant from center: Let distance from O to each chord
= d
If chords are on same side: not possible (they would coincide) If chords are on opposite sides: OM + ON = 5 cm
By symmetry for equal chords: OM = ON = 2.5 cm
Let AB = 2x (so AM = x) In triangle OAM: OA² = OM² + AM² 13² = 2.5² + x² 169 = 6.25 + x² x²
= 162.75 x = 12.76 cm
Length of chord AB = 2x = 2(12.76) = 25.52 cm
Example 5: In a circle, chord PQ subtends an angle of 60° at the center. If the radius is 8 cm, find the length of the chord.
Diagram 41:

P
•
|\
| \
8 | \ 8
|60°\
| /|\
| / | \
|/ | \ O•	|	•Q
M

Solution: Draw OM perpendicular to PQ (M is midpoint) In triangle OMP: ∠POM = 60°/2 = 30° (OM bisects angle)
Using trigonometry: PM/OP = sin 30° PM/8 = 0.5 PM = 4 cm Length of chord PQ = 2 × PM = 2 × 4 = 8 cm
Alternatively, triangle OPQ is isosceles with OP = OQ = 8 cm and ∠POQ = 60° This makes it equilateral (since base angles = (180° - 60°)/2 = 60°) Therefore PQ = 8 cm
Example 6: In the diagram, ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral with ∠BAD = 3x and ∠BCD = 2x
+ 20°. Find the value of x and all angles.
Solution: Since ABCD is cyclic: ∠BAD + ∠BCD = 180° (opposite angles supplementary) 3x + 2x + 20° = 180° 5x = 160° x = 32°
Therefore: ∠BAD = 3(32°) = 96° ∠BCD = 2(32°) + 20° = 84°
Also, ∠ABC + ∠ADC = 180° We would need more information to find individual values of
∠ABC and ∠ADC.
Example 7: Two chords AB and CD of a circle intersect at right angles at point E inside the circle. If AE = 3 cm, EB = 4 cm, and CE = 2 cm, find ED.

Solution: When two chords intersect inside a circle: AE × EB = CE × ED (intersecting chords theorem)
3 × 4 = 2 × ED 12 = 2 × ED ED = 6 cm
Class Activity:
Activity 1: Draw a circle and construct two equal chords. Measure their distances from the center to verify they are equal.
Activity 2: Draw a circle and mark an arc. Measure the angle at the center and at three different points on the circumference to verify the relationship.
Activity 3: Construct a cyclic quadrilateral and measure opposite angles to verify they sum to 180°.
Evaluation Questions:
A chord of length 24 cm is 5 cm from the center of a circle. Find the radius of the circle.

An arc subtends an angle of 120° at the center of a circle. What angle does it subtend at the circumference?
In a cyclic quadrilateral PQRS, ∠P = 110° and ∠R = 70°. Find ∠Q and ∠S.
Two equal chords of a circle are 8 cm apart and each is 12 cm long. Find the radius if the chords are on opposite sides of the center.
State the relationship between equal chords and their distances from the center of a circle.

In a circle with center O and radius 10 cm, a chord AB is 6 cm from the center. Calculate: (a) The length of the chord (b) The angle subtended by the chord at the center
ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral where ∠A = 2x + 15° and ∠C = 3x - 10°. Find: (a) The value of x (b) All four angles of the quadrilateral if ∠B = 90°
In a circle, two chords AB and CD are equal. If the perpendicular distance from the center to AB is 4 cm and the radius is 5 cm, find the length of each chord.
An arc of a circle subtends an angle of 80° at a point P on the circumference. Find: (a) The angle at the center
The angle at another point Q on the major arc

Draw a circle of radius 6 cm and construct two chords each of length 10 cm. Measure and calculate their distances from the center.

WEEK 4: CIRCLE GEOMETRY III
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
Apply the theorem of angles in the same segment
Verify that angle in a semicircle is 90°
Use exterior angle properties of cyclic quadrilaterals
Solve complex problems combining multiple circle theorems
Prove circle geometry theorems using logical reasoning
Entry Behaviour:
Students understand properties of chords, tangents, angles at center and circumference, and cyclic quadrilaterals.
Instructional Materials:
Compass and ruler
Protractor
Circle theorem summary charts
String or wire
Whiteboard and markers
Prepared circle diagrams
Calculator
Content:
Angles in the Same Segment
Theorem: Angles in the same segment of a circle are equal.
Definition: A segment is the region between a chord and an arc. Angles in the same segment are angles subtended by the same chord (or arc) at different points on the same arc.

Diagram 43: Angles in Same Segment

∠APB = ∠AQB = ∠ARB = α
(All angles in the same segment)

Points P, Q, R lie on the same arc (major arc) All subtend the same chord AB
Therefore, all angles are equal

∠APB = α (angle in major segment)
∠AQB = β (angle in minor segment)
α + β = 180° (angles on opposite sides of chord)

Key Points:
Angles subtended by the same arc at the circumference are equal
This applies to any number of points on the same arc
Angles in opposite segments are supplementary (add to 180°)
Angle in a Semicircle (Detailed Study)
Theorem: The angle inscribed in a semicircle is a right angle (90°).
Formal Statement: If AB is a diameter of a circle and P is any point on the circle (not A or B), then ∠APB = 90°.
Diagram 45: Proof of Angle in Semicircle

P
•
/|\
/ | \
/α |β \

\	|	/
\ θ | θ /
\	|	/
\ 2θ| /
\ | /
\|/
•O (Center)

Proof outline:
Draw OP (radius)
OA = OP = OB = r (all radii)
Triangle OAP is isosceles: ∠OAP = ∠OPA = α
Triangle OBP is isosceles: ∠OBP = ∠OPB = β
∠AOB = 180° (straight line - diameter)
In triangle AOB: 2α + 2β = 180°
Therefore: α + β = 90°
So ∠APB = α + β = 90°

Diagram 46: Multiple Positions in Semicircle

O• (Center)

All marked angles = 90°
This is true for ANY point on the semicircle

Converse: If the angle subtended by a chord at a point on the circle is 90°, then the chord is a diameter.
Applications:
Finding right angles in circles
Constructing perpendicular lines
Solving geometric problems
Architecture and engineering
Exterior Angle Properties
Theorem 1: The exterior angle of a cyclic quadrilateral equals the interior opposite angle.

∠BCD + ∠DCE = 180° (linear pair)
∠BCD + ∠DAB = 180° (opposite angles of cyclic quad) Therefore: ∠DCE = ∠DAB

Problem-Solving Strategy:
Identify all given information
Mark known angles and lengths on diagram
Identify which theorems apply
Apply theorems systematically
Use properties of triangles and quadrilaterals
Verify answer makes sense
Worked Examples:
Example 1: In a circle with center O, points P, Q, and R lie on the circumference. If ∠POQ = 80° and chord PQ is extended to meet chord QR, find ∠PQR.

Solution: ∠POQ = 80° (angle at center)
Angle at circumference = ½ × angle at center ∠PRQ = ½ × 80° = 40° (angle in same segment as POQ)
In triangle PQR: We need more information to find ∠PQR completely. However, if R is positioned such that PR is a diameter: ∠PQR = 90° (angle in semicircle)
Example 2: ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral. If ∠ABC = 75° and ∠BCD = 110°, find: (a)
∠ADC (b) ∠DAB (c) The exterior angle at A when AB is produced

Diagram 51:

Solution: (a) ∠ADC + ∠ABC = 180° (opposite angles of cyclic quadrilateral) ∠ADC + 75° = 180° ∠ADC = 105°
∠DAB + ∠BCD = 180° (opposite angles of cyclic quadrilateral) ∠DAB + 110° = 180°
∠DAB = 70°
Exterior angle at A (∠EAD) = ∠BCD = 110° (exterior angle equals interior opposite angle)
Example 3: In a circle, AB is a diameter and C is a point on the circle such that AC = BC. If AB
= 10 cm, find: (a) ∠ACB (b) The length of AC
Diagram 52:

C
•
/|\

/90°|	\
/	|	\
/	|	\ A•	|		•B
O• (Center)
AB = 10 cm

Solution: (a) Since AB is diameter: ∠ACB = 90° (angle in semicircle)
(b) Triangle ABC is right-angled at C with AC = BC (given) Therefore, triangle ABC is isosceles right-angled
Using Pythagoras: AC² + BC² = AB² Since AC = BC: 2AC² = 10²
2AC² = 100
AC² = 50
AC = √50 = 5√2 ≈ 7.07 cm

Example 4: Points P, Q, R, and S lie on a circle. If ∠PQR = 35° and ∠QRS = 55°, find ∠RPS and ∠PSQ.
Diagram 53:
P•	•Q
|\	/|
| \ 35°/ |
| \ /	|
|	\/	|
|	/\	|
| / \	|
| / 55°\ |
|/	\ |
S•	•R
Solution: ∠PQR = 35° (given) ∠PSR = 35° (angles in same segment - both subtend arc PR)
∠QRS = 55° (given) ∠QPS = 55° (angles in same segment - both subtend arc QS) To find ∠RPS: ∠RPS is part of ∠QPS We need to find ∠QPR
Since PQRS is cyclic: ∠PQR + ∠PSR = not necessarily 180° (they're not opposite angles)
In triangle PQR: ∠QPR + ∠PQR + ∠QRP = 180° ∠QPR + 35° + (part of angle at R) = 180° Without additional information, we cannot determine exact value.
Example 5: A tangent PT touches a circle at T. Chord TA is drawn such that ∠PTA = 42°. If B is another point on the circle on the major arc, find ∠TBA.

Diagram 54:

Solution: Using alternate segment theorem: ∠PTA = ∠TBA (angle between tangent and chord equals angle in alternate segment)
Therefore: ∠TBA = 42°
Example 6: In the diagram, O is the center of the circle, ∠ABC = 55°, and BC is a diameter. Find: (a) ∠BAC (b) ∠AOC (c) ∠OAC
Diagram 55:

/55°|	\
/	|	\ B•	|		•C
O• (Center) Diameter BC
Solution: (a) Since BC is diameter: ∠BAC = 90° (angle in semicircle)
In triangle ABC: ∠ABC + ∠BAC + ∠ACB = 180° 55° + 90° + ∠ACB = 180° ∠ACB = 35°
∠AOC = 2 × ∠ABC = 2 × 55° = 110°
(angle at center = 2 × angle at circumference)

Triangle OAC is isosceles (OA = OC = radius) ∠OAC = ∠OCA
In triangle OAC:
∠AOC + ∠OAC + ∠OCA = 180° 110° + 2∠OAC = 180°
2∠OAC = 70°
∠OAC = 35°

Example 7: PQRS is a cyclic quadrilateral with PQ = QR. If ∠QPS = 35° and ∠PRS = 40°, find ∠PQR.
Diagram 56:

Q

\	O•	/
\	|	/

• S
Solution: ∠QPS = 35° (given) ∠QRS = 35° (angles in same segment, subtending arc QS)
∠PRS = 40° (given) ∠PQS = 40° (angles in same segment, subtending arc PS)
In triangle PQR: Since PQ = QR (given), triangle PQR is isosceles Let ∠QPR = ∠QRP = x
∠PQR + 2x = 180° (sum of angles in triangle)
From cyclic quadrilateral property: ∠QPS + ∠PRS = part of opposite angles
∠PQR = ∠PQS + ∠SQR = 40° + angle needed
Actually, ∠PRQ = ∠PRS + ∠SRQ = 40° + 35° = 75° Since PQ = QR: ∠QPR = ∠QRP = 75° In triangle PQR: ∠PQR = 180° - 75° - 75° = 30°
Class Activity:
Activity 1: Draw a circle and mark four points on it. Draw different chords and measure angles in the same segment to verify they are equal.
Activity 2: Construct a semicircle and mark 5 different points on it. Measure the angles to verify they are all 90°.
Activity 3: Draw a cyclic quadrilateral, produce one side, and measure the exterior angle and interior opposite angle to verify they are equal.
Evaluation Questions:
Points A, B, and C lie on a circle. If ∠ABC = 48°, what is the angle subtended by arc AC at another point D on the major arc?
In a circle with diameter PQ, if point R is on the circle and ∠PRQ = 90°, what can you conclude about PQ?
ABCD is a cyclic quadrilateral with ∠ABC = 95°. When BC is produced to E, find ∠DCE.
In a circle, chord AB subtends an angle of 40° at point C on the major arc. Find the angle at the center O.
State the relationship between angles in the same segment of a circle.

In a circle with center O, ∠AOB = 140°. Points P and Q lie on the major arc AB. Find: (a) ∠APB (b) ∠AQB
(c) What can you say about ∠APB and ∠AQB?

PQRS is a cyclic quadrilateral where ∠PQR = 105° and ∠QRS = 85°. Find: (a) ∠RSP (b) ∠SPQ (c) The exterior angle when PQ is produced beyond Q
In a circle, AB is a diameter and C is a point on the circle. If ∠CAB = 32°, find: (a) ∠ACB (b) ∠ABC (c)
∠AOC (where O is the center)

A tangent to a circle at point T makes an angle of 58° with chord TA. Point B is on the major arc. Find ∠TBA using the alternate segment theorem.
Three points P, Q, and R lie on a circle such that PQ = PR. If ∠PQR = 65°, find: (a) ∠PRQ (b) ∠QPR (c) The angle subtended by arc QR at the center

WEEK 6: TRIGONOMETRY I
Learning Objectives:
By the end of the lesson, students should be able to:
State and apply the sine rule
State and apply the cosine rule
Solve triangles using sine and cosine rules
Determine when to use each rule
Apply trigonometric rules to real-life problems
Entry Behaviour:
Students understand basic trigonometric ratios (sin, cos, tan) and can work with angles and triangles.
Instructional Materials:
Calculator (scientific)
Protractor and ruler
Triangle models (various types)
Trigonometry formula charts
Whiteboard and markers
Real-life application pictures
Content:
Review of Triangle Properties
Types of Triangles:
